)) that neatly cancels this determinant. Consequently, we demonstrate the equivalence between the generating functionals without requiring any additional condition. This reinforces our interpretation of the determinant in Eq. (
234
) as the Senjanović determinant, which arises due to the auxiliary field
G^{\lambda}{}_{\mu\nu}
. The resulting generating functional is

(236)

\begin{split}Z_{\text{HP}}[j,J]={}&N\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi^{\mu\nu}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}d_{\nu}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{d}_{\mu}}\exp i\int\mathop{d^{D}x}\bigg({\mathcal{L}}_{\text{eff}}^{\text{II}}+j_{\mu\nu}\phi^{\mu\nu}+{J}_{\lambda}^{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}(h)h^{\pi\tau}_{,\rho}\\
&-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}J^{\mu\nu}_{\lambda}\mathchoice{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}(h)J^{\pi\tau}_{\rho}\bigg).\end{split}

Note that the action in the above expression is the SOGR action in Eq. (
128
). Besides that, the source term is rather involved and unusual as we see a quadratic dependence on
J
.

When
J=0
,

(237)

Z_{\text{HP}}[j,0]=Z_{\text{HE}}[j],

which shows transparently the equivalence between these formalisms.
Consequently,

(238)

\begin{split}\langle 0|T\phi^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}(x_{1})\cdots\phi^{{\mu_{n}\nu_{n}}}(x_{n})|0\rangle&\equiv\left.(-i)^{n}\frac{\delta^{n}Z_{\text{HE}}[j]}{\delta j_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}(x_{1})\cdots\delta{j}_{\mu_{n}\nu_{n}}(x_{n})}\right|_{j=0}\\
&=\left.(-i)^{n}\frac{\delta^{n}Z_{\text{HP}}[j,J]}{\delta j_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}(x_{1})\cdots\delta{j}_{\mu_{n}\nu_{n}}(x_{n})}\right|_{J=j=0}.\end{split}

When
J\neq 0
, the functional derivatives with respect to
J^{\mu\nu}_{\lambda}
of the right-hand side of Eq. (
236
) leads to Green’s functions of the composite field
\mathbb{G}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}(x)
computed in the SOGR formulation, which is the classical value of the auxiliary field
G_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)
. The same functional derivative in the left-hand side of the same expression would lead to Green’s functions of the auxiliary field
G_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}
. One can easily verify that the following structural identities can be derived:

(239a)

\displaystyle\langle 0|TG_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)\phi^{\alpha\beta}(y)|0\rangle=\kappa\langle 0|T\mathchoice{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}[\eta+\kappa\phi(x)]\phi^{\pi\tau}_{,\rho}(x)\phi^{\alpha\beta}(y)|0\rangle,

(239b)

\displaystyle\langle 0|TG_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)G_{\alpha\beta}^{\gamma}(y)|0\rangle=i\kappa^{2}\langle 0|T\mathchoice{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\gamma}\kern 5.53265pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\alpha\beta}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\gamma}\kern 5.53265pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\alpha\beta}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\gamma}\kern 4.78525pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\alpha\beta}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\gamma}\kern 4.78525pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\alpha\beta}}}}[\eta+\kappa\phi(x)]|0\rangle\delta(x-y)

\displaystyle+\kappa^{2}\langle 0|T\mathchoice{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho_{2}}\kern 8.11803pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi_{2}\tau_{2}}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho_{2}}\kern 8.11803pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi_{2}\tau_{2}}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho_{2}}\kern 7.83636pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi_{2}\tau_{2}}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho_{2}}\kern 7.83636pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi_{2}\tau_{2}}}}}[\eta+\kappa\phi(x)]\phi^{\pi_{2}\tau_{2}}_{,\rho_{2}}(x)\mathchoice{(M^{-1})^{{{\gamma}\kern 5.53265pt{\rho_{1}}\kern 8.11803pt}}_{{{\alpha\beta}{\pi_{1}\tau_{1}}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\gamma}\kern 5.53265pt{\rho_{1}}\kern 8.11803pt}}_{{{\alpha\beta}{\pi_{1}\tau_{1}}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\gamma}\kern 4.78525pt{\rho_{1}}\kern 7.83636pt}}_{{{\alpha\beta}{\pi_{1}\tau_{1}}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\gamma}\kern 4.78525pt{\rho_{1}}\kern 7.83636pt}}_{{{\alpha\beta}{\pi_{1}\tau_{1}}}}}[\eta+\kappa\phi(y)]\phi^{\pi_{1}\tau_{1}}_{,\rho_{1}}(y)|0\rangle.

These identities are analog of the Eqs. (
178b
) and (
178c
), respectively.

9.2
Diagonal first-order formulation

The quantum equivalence of the DFOGR formulation is immediate.
Consider the shift

(240)

{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}\rightarrow\mathring{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}+\mathbb{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}-\kappa^{2}\mathchoice{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}(\eta+\kappa\phi){J}_{\rho}^{\pi\tau},

where

(241)

\mathbb{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)\equiv\mathchoice{[M^{-1}(h)-M^{-1}(\eta)]^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{[M^{-1}(h)-M^{-1}(\eta)]^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{[M^{-1}(h)-M^{-1}(\eta)]^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{[M^{-1}(h)-M^{-1}(\eta)]^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}h^{\pi\tau}_{,\rho}(x)

is the classical value of the field
H_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}
. Upon this shift, the generating functional of the DFOGR (
168
) can be rewritten as

(242)

\begin{split}Z^{\text{Id}}_{\text{HP}}={}&N\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi^{\mu\nu}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}d_{\nu}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{d}_{\mu}}\exp i\int\mathop{d^{D}x}\Big(\mathcal{L}_{\textrm{eff}}^{\textrm{II}}+\kappa{J}_{\lambda}^{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{[M^{-1}(\eta+\kappa\phi)-M^{-1}(\eta)]^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{[M^{-1}(\eta+\kappa\phi)-M^{-1}(\eta)]^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{[M^{-1}(\eta+\kappa\phi)-M^{-1}(\eta)]^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{[M^{-1}(\eta+\kappa\phi)-M^{-1}(\eta)]^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}\phi^{\pi\tau}_{,\rho}\\
&-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}{J}_{\lambda}^{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}(\eta+\kappa\phi){J}_{\rho}^{\pi\tau}+{j}_{\mu\nu}\phi^{\mu\nu}\Big).\end{split}

Likewise the HP formalism, we have that

(243)

Z_{\text{HE}}^{\text{Id}}[j,0]=Z_{\text{HE}}^{\text{II}}[j].

When
J\neq 0
, we can obtain structural identities by applying functional derivatives on both sides of Eq. (
242
).
For instance, the functional derivative with respect to
J^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}
yields

(244)

\langle 0|TH_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)|0\rangle=\langle 0|T\mathbb{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)|0\rangle.

Note that the right-hand side can be computed in both the FOGR and SOGR formulation. Thus, this identity can be seen as an identity between Green’s functions of the FOGR theory. Consequently, it is the quantum realization of the classical equation of motion of
H_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}
(
241
).

We also have
[
10
]

(245)

\langle 0|TH_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)\phi^{\pi\tau}(y)|0\rangle=\langle 0|T\mathbb{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)\phi^{\pi\tau}(y)|0\rangle

and

(246)

\langle 0|TH_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)H_{\pi\tau}^{\rho}(y)|0\rangle=\langle 0|T\mathbb{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)\mathbb{H}_{\pi\tau}^{\rho}(y)|0\rangle+i\kappa^{2}\delta(x-y)\langle 0|T\mathchoice{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}[\eta+\kappa\phi(x)]|0\rangle.

The contact term in the above expression arises due to the quadratic dependence on
J
in Eq. (
242
). These identities relate the FOGR Green’s function with Green’s function of the composite field (
241
) computed in the SOGR.

One can easily generalize these identities, for example:

(247)

\langle 0|TH_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)\phi^{\pi_{1}\tau_{1}}(y_{1})\cdots\phi^{\pi_{n}\tau_{n}}(y_{n})|0\rangle=\langle 0|T\mathbb{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)\phi^{\pi_{1}\tau_{1}}(y_{1})\cdots\phi^{\pi_{n}\tau_{n}}(y_{n})|0\rangle.

Moreover, we can obtain identities between the FOGR and the DFOGR formulations, such as

(248)

\begin{split}\langle 0|TG_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)\phi^{\alpha\beta}(y)|0\rangle-\langle 0|TH_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)\phi^{\alpha\beta}(y)|0\rangle={}&\langle 0|T[\mathbb{G}-\mathbb{H}]\mathchoice{{}^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{{}^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{{}^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{{}^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}(x)\phi^{\alpha\beta}(y)|0\rangle\\
={}&\langle 0|T\mathchoice{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}(\eta)h^{\pi\tau}_{,\rho}(x)\phi^{\alpha\beta}(y)|0\rangle,\end{split}

which follows from the definition of the classical values (see Eqs. (
141a
) and (
241
)).

9.3
Structural identities at one-loop order

The identities (
245
) and (
246
) are rewritten as

(249a)

\displaystyle\langle 0|T{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x){\phi}^{\pi\tau}(y)|0\rangle={}

\displaystyle\langle 0|T\mathbb{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x){\phi}^{\pi\tau}(y)|0\rangle,

(249b)

\displaystyle\langle 0|T{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x){H}_{\pi\tau}^{\rho}(y)|0\rangle={}

\displaystyle i\kappa^{2}\langle 0|T\mathchoice{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}[h(x)]|0\rangle\,\delta(x-y)+\langle 0|T\mathbb{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}(x)\mathbb{H}_{\pi\tau}^{\rho}(y)|0\rangle,

where
\mathbb{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}
is the composite field defined in Eq. (
241
).
Using the expansion

(250)

M^{-1}(\eta+\kappa\phi)-m^{-1}=-\kappa m^{-1}M(\phi)m^{-1}+\kappa^{2}m^{-1}M(\phi)m^{-1}M(\phi)m^{-1}+\cdots,

where

(251)

m\equiv M(\eta),

one easily verify Eqs. (
249a
) and (
249b
) at the tree-level.
For example, the right-hand side of the identity (
249b
) yields
i\kappa^{2}m^{-1}
, which is equal to the propagator of the auxiliary field
H_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}
(see Eq. (
432
)). The only contribution from the left-hand side comes from the contact term,
i\kappa^{2}m^{-1}
, as expected. At one-loop order pinched diagrams must be considered which leads to an involved algebraic computation. Here we show that we can verify these identities even at the integrand level extending the approach used for YM theory to gravity.

The one-loop contributions to the left-hand side of the structural identities Eqs. (
249a
) and (
249b
) are shown in Fig.
5
.

Figure 5:
In diagram (a), we have the one-loop contribution to the mixed propagator
\langle 0|TH^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}\phi^{\pi\tau}|0\rangle
. In diagram (b), we have the one-loop contribution to the propagator
\left\langle 0|TH_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}H_{\pi\tau}^{\rho}|0\right\rangle
. The momenta satisfy the condition
q=p+k
.

In
[
10
,
11
]
, we give a thorough examination of these Green’s functions. We have obtained the one-loop contributions for any dimension
D
and any gauge parameter
\alpha
. Before we proceed, note that the tadpole-like contribution shown in Fig.
6
is canceled by the Senjanović determinant that we proposed in Eq. (
234
). Thus, we do not have to appeal to any particular regularization scheme to show that these contributions must vanish. This statement also holds when we show the quantum equivalence of the FOGR and the SOGR generating functionals.

Figure 6:
Tadpole-like contributions coming from the naive FOGR path integral (without the Senjanović determinant).

Now, we have to consider the pinched diagrams arising from the right-hand side of these structural identities.
The right-hand side of Eq. (
249a
), at one-loop order (
\kappa^{2}
), becomes

(252)

-\kappa^{2}\langle 0|T\mathchoice{[m^{-1}M(\phi)m^{-1}]^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\gamma}\kern 5.53265pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\alpha\beta}}}}{[m^{-1}M(\phi)m^{-1}]^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\gamma}\kern 5.53265pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\alpha\beta}}}}{[m^{-1}M(\phi)m^{-1}]^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\gamma}\kern 4.78525pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\alpha\beta}}}}{[m^{-1}M(\phi)m^{-1}]^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\gamma}\kern 4.78525pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\alpha\beta}}}}(x)\phi^{\alpha\beta}_{,\gamma}(x)\phi^{\pi\tau}(y)|0\rangle,

where the expression (
250
) is used.

Accordingly to
[
10
]
, we define

(253)

\kappa\mathfrak{M}{}{}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}{}_{\alpha\beta}^{\gamma}{}_{\rho\sigma}\phi^{\rho\sigma}\equiv-\kappa\mathchoice{[m^{-1}M(\phi)m^{-1}]^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\gamma}\kern 5.53265pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\alpha\beta}}}}{[m^{-1}M(\phi)m^{-1}]^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\gamma}\kern 5.53265pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\alpha\beta}}}}{[m^{-1}M(\phi)m^{-1}]^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\gamma}\kern 4.78525pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\alpha\beta}}}}{[m^{-1}M(\phi)m^{-1}]^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\gamma}\kern 4.78525pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\alpha\beta}}}}

and rewrite Eq. (
252
) as

(254)

\kappa^{2}\mathfrak{M}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}{}_{\alpha\beta}^{\gamma}{}_{\rho\sigma}\langle 0|T\phi^{\rho\sigma}(x)\phi^{\alpha\beta}_{,\gamma}(x)\phi^{\pi\tau}(y)|0\rangle.

This can be computed using the same limiting procedure as for the pinched diagrams in YM theory. On the other hand, amputating each graviton propagator from the following structural identity

(255)

\langle 0|T\mathbb{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}\phi^{\alpha\beta}\phi^{\sigma\omega}|0\rangle=\langle 0|TH_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}\phi^{\alpha\beta}\phi^{\sigma\omega}|0\rangle

results in

(256)

(\mathbb{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}\phi^{\alpha\beta}\phi^{\sigma\omega})={i\kappa^{2}\mathchoice{(m^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(m^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(m^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(m^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}}(H^{\pi\tau}_{\rho}\phi^{\alpha\beta}\phi^{\sigma\omega}),

where
i\kappa^{2}\mathchoice{(m^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(m^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(m^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{(m^{-1})^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}
is the free propagator
\langle 0|TH_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}H_{\pi\tau}^{\rho}|0\rangle_{\text{free}}
. At tree-level, Eq. (
256
) determines the extended Feynman rule for the composite field
\mathbb{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}
, which is shown in Fig.
7
.

Figure 7:
Extended Feynman rule for the interaction vertex with a composite field
\mathbb{H}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}
, which is represented by a double wavy line. The definition of
V_{\rho}^{\pi\tau}{}^{\alpha\beta\sigma\omega}(p,q,k)
is given in Eq. (
434
).

Using this extended rule, we can easily compute the pinched diagrams in gravity. However, this is not necessary. From Eq. (
256
) can be inferred that the pinched diagrams in Figs
8
and
9
should be equivalent to diagrams in the FOGR formalism (see Fig.
10
). In Fig.
10
, we observe that each diagram on the left-hand side is equivalent to the respective diagram on the right-hand side.
Thus, we verify the structural identities (
249a
) and (
249b
) at one-loop order at the integrand level.

Figure 8:
Pinched diagram that contributes to the right-hand side of Eq. (
249a
) at one-loop order. We also present the same diagram using the extended Feynman rule in Fig.
7
.

Figure 9:
Pinched diagram, at one-loop order, which contributes to the right-hand side of Eq. (
249b
). We also present the same diagrams using the extended Feynman rule in Fig.
7
.

We shall remark that this is only possible when we consider the proper path integral for the first-order formulation of gravity. The determinant in Eq. (
234
) is responsible for the cancellation of spurious contributions, such as the tadpole-like contributions in Fig.
6
.
In
[
11
,
10
]
these diagrams were not relevant since dimensional regularization was employed to compute them.
By using different schemes, or at finite temperature, these diagrams could possibly give a non-vanishing contribution (see Appendix
12
). This possibility could reveal the inequivalence between the FOGR and the SOGR formalisms.

In this work, we addressed this issue by accounting for the second-class constraints in the FOGR leading to the proposed Senjanović determinant in Eq. (
154
).
This determinant is not only consistent with the results presented in this section but also essential for verifying the structural identities at the integrand level.
Moreover, it is necessary for establishing the quantum equivalence, as we have demonstrated in the previous section and confirmed here through a diagrammatic analysis.

[Structural identity (
249a
). ]

\subbottom
[Structural identity (
249b
).]

Figure 10:
Diagrammatic representation of the structural identities at one-loop order. Pinched diagrams are fully equivalent to FOGR diagrams.

Chapter 5
Lagrange multiplier formalism

In this chapter, we present the
standard Lagrange multiplier formalism

[
27
,
8
,
28
,
29
,
30
,
31
,
32
]
in which LM fields are introduced to restrict the path integral to fields configurations that satisfy the classical equations of motion. In this formalism, the radiative corrections of orders higher than one-loop are suppressed. The resulting path integral describes a solvable theory, which, in general, is renormalizable. Moreover, it is consistent with the unitary condition
[
31
]
. On the other hand, the degrees of freedom are doubled with the presence of the LM fields. These LM fields contribute at one-loop order doubling the usual one-loop contribution. In the next chapter, we consider a extension of this formalism in which this doubling is absent
[
37
]
.

Clearly, this formalism is not suited to the Standard Model since higher-loop effects are required to describe nature well. For example, the computation of the electron anomalous magnetic moment at to tenth-order
[
78
]
or the lambda shift at third-order
[
79
]
shows the relevance of higher-order loops to obtain more accurate theoretical values which reflects the results of the experiments
[
80
,
81
]
. On the other side, it is perfectly suited as a quantum gravity alternative. The formulation of gravity in this formalism has GR as its classical limit and is renormalizable.

It is interesting to remark that this formalism, proposed by McKeon and Sherry
[
27
]
, is a generalization of the structure of lower dimensional gravity theories, in which fields appear linearly, to
(3+1)
-gravity. These fields play the role of LM fields imposing the equations of motion. Let us take, for example,
(2+1)
-dimensional gravity. The HE action in three dimensions is
[
82
]

(257)

S_{3\text{HE}}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{M}\epsilon^{ijk}\epsilon_{ABC}\mathchoice{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\left(\partial_{j}\mathchoice{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{BC}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.81512pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.58223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displayst where

\mathchoice{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{BC}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.81512pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.58223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{BC}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.81512pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.58223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{BC}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.81512pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.58223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{BC}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.81512pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.58223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}

(258)

\omega_{j}^{AB}=\frac{1}{2}e^{iA}\left(\mathchoice{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.3432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.3432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.65945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.61388pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{,j}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.3432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.3432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.65945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.61388pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{,j}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.3432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.3432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.65945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.61388pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{,j}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.3432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.3432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.65945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makeb In Eq. (
257
), it is the vierbein
\mathchoice{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
that appears linearly. It imposes the Einstein equations in the vacuum
\mathchoice{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{BC}}}_{{{jk}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.81512pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.58223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{BC}}}_{{{jk}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.81512pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.58223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{BC}}}_{{{jk}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.81512pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.58223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{BC}}}_{{{jk}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.60898pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.81512pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.58223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=0
, with no cosmological constant
\Lambda=0
. In
1+1
dimensional gravity, it is the dilaton field
\phi
that appears linearly. This is a scalar field that imposes the two-dimensional Einstein equations
R+\Lambda=0

[
84
]
, where
R=g^{\mu\nu}R_{\mu\nu}
is the Ricci scalar.

In
(3+1)
-dimensional gravity, or simply GR, this structure does not emerge naturally from its Lagrangian formulation. Thus, the standard LM formalism consists of imposing the equations of motion by introducing a new field
\lambda_{\mu\nu}
. The HE action is supplemented with the LM action

(259)

S_{\lambda}=\int\mathop{d^{4}x}\sqrt{-g}\lambda_{\mu\nu}\left(R^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}Rg^{\mu\nu}\right)

so that
\lambda_{\mu\nu}
can act similarly to the dilation in two-dimensional gravity (or the vierbein in Eq. (
257
)).

10
General formalism

In this section, we describe the LM formalism in general terms. For this, we consider a scalar theory with
N
commuting fields
\phi_{i}
. This theory is described by a non-singular action

(260)

S[\phi]=\int\mathop{dx}\mathcal{L}(\phi).

The indices will be omitted for simplicity. The equations of motion are derived through Hamilton’s principle which reads

(261)

\frac{\delta S[\phi]}{\delta\phi}=0.

Since we assumed that the action
S[\phi]
is non-singular, that is, the system is not constrained, the quantization within the path integral formalism is straightforward. The generating functional of the general action in Eq. (
260
) is

(262)

Z[j]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi}\exp{i\int\mathop{dx}\left(\mathcal{L}(\phi)+j\phi\right)}.

In the framework of the standard LM formalism, we supplement the action Eq. (
260
) with

(263)

S_{\lambda}=\int\mathop{dx}\lambda\frac{\delta S[\phi]}{\delta\phi}.

At the classical level, the field
\lambda
plays the role of an LM which imposes the equations of motion as a constraint. In section
12
, we investigate the classical behavior of this system in more detail. Now, we shall proceed by presenting the standard quantization of the LM theory obtained in the LM formalism.

The standard quantization procedure leads to the generating functional

(264)

\mathsf{Z}_{\text{LM}}[j,k]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda}\exp{i\int\mathop{dx}\left[\mathcal{L}(\phi)+\lambda\left(\frac{\delta S}{\delta\phi}+j\right)+j\phi+k\lambda\right]}.

Note that the LM field
\lambda
couples to both sources
j
and
k
. This is required as the sources should be introduced in the equations of motion. Or equivalently, the action in Eq. (
263
) must include the sources.

In section
10.2
, we show that the standard LM formalism can be extended to fermionic systems.

10.1
Perturbative properties

One can integrate the LM field
\lambda
in Eq. (
264
) which leads to

(265)

\mathsf{Z}_{\text{LM}}[j,k]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi}\mathop{\delta}\left(\frac{\delta S}{\delta\phi}+j+k\right)\exp{i\int\mathop{dx}\left[\mathcal{L}(\phi)+j\phi\right]}.

Now, it is transparent that the LM fields
\lambda
constraints the path integral to field configurations
\tilde{\phi}(j,k)
that satisfy

(266)

\left.\frac{\delta S}{\delta\phi}\right|_{\phi=\tilde{\phi}}+j+k=0.

We can try to integrate the field
\phi
in Eq. (
265
) using the functional analog of the following property of the
\delta
-function

(267)

\delta\left(f(x)\right)=\sum_{i}|f^{\prime}(\tilde{x}_{i})|^{-1}\delta\left(x-\tilde{x}_{i}\right),

where
\tilde{x}_{i}
satisfy
f(\tilde{x}_{i})=0
. In our case, it becomes

(268)

\mathop{\delta}\left(\frac{\delta S}{\delta\phi}+j+k\right)=\sum_{\tilde{\phi}}\left|\det\frac{\delta^{2}S[\tilde{\phi}]}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\right|^{-1}\mathop{\delta}\left(\phi-\tilde{\phi}\right).

We assume that the Hessian of the action
S[\phi]
is always positive. Hence, the absolute value can be discarded.

Replacing Eq. (
268
) in Eq. (
265
) yields

(269)

\mathsf{Z}_{\text{LM }}[j,k]=\sum_{\tilde{\phi}}\det\left(\frac{\delta^{2}S[\tilde{\phi}]}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\right)^{-1}\exp i\left(S[\tilde{\phi}]+\int\mathop{dx}j\tilde{\phi}(j,k)\right).

Thus, we obtained an exact form for the generating functional in the LM formalism. This shows that the resulting theory is solvable. The dependence on the source
k
is entirely on
\tilde{\phi}(j,k)
.

The exponential in Eq. (
269
) leads to tree-level diagrams, while the determinant is the square of the usual one-loop determinant
\det[\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\phi)]^{-1/2}
. Thus, the tree-level is kept unaltered. However, the one-loop contributions comes out twice the usual obtained from Eq. (
262
). The higher-order loop diagrams are absent.

This result also can be seen in a diagrammatic way
[
29
,
37
]
. Expanding the Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}(\phi)
as

(270)

\mathcal{L}(\phi)=\frac{1}{2}a_{ij}\phi_{i}\phi_{j}+\frac{1}{3!}a_{ijk}\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\phi_{k}+\frac{1}{4!}a_{ijkl}\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\phi_{k}\phi_{l}+\cdots,

the Lagrangian in the framework of the standard LM theory is

(271)

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\text{LM}}(\phi)={}&\frac{1}{2}a_{ij}\phi_{i}\phi_{j}+\frac{1}{3!}a_{ijk}\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\phi_{k}+\frac{1}{4!}a_{ijkl}\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\phi_{k}\phi_{l}+\cdots\\
&+a_{ij}\lambda_{i}\phi_{j}+\frac{1}{2}a_{ijk}\lambda_{i}\phi_{j}\phi_{k}+\frac{1}{3!}a_{ijkl}\lambda_{i}\phi_{j}\phi_{k}\phi_{l}+\cdots.\end{split}

We can read off the Feynman rules from the above expression straightforwardly. The propagators are given by the inverse of the matrix

(272)

\begin{pmatrix}a_{ij}&a_{ij}\\
a_{ij}&0\end{pmatrix}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}0&a_{ij}^{-1}\\
a_{ij}^{-1}&-a^{-1}_{ij}\end{pmatrix}.

Thus, we have the mixed propagators
\langle\lambda_{i}\phi_{j}\rangle=\langle\phi_{i}\lambda_{j}\rangle=a_{ij}^{-1}
and the propagator of the LM field
\langle\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}\rangle=-a_{ij}^{-1}
. Note that, the physical field
\phi
does not propagate anymore.

Besides that, the negative sign in the propagator of the LM fields suggests the presence of instabilities (ghosts) in the theory
[
27
,
37
]
.
The origins of the instabilities in the LM theory have not yet been fully established. We interpret them as Ostrogradsky instabilities
[
33
]
.
10
10

10

Ostrogradsky instabilities are related to the presence of higher derivative terms in the Lagrangian. In the LM formalism, the equations of motion, usually of second-order, are introduced in the Lagrangian using an LM field.
For clarity, we illustrate this argument by using an example in section
12
, which can be generalized to any field theory in a straightforward manner.

We already know that:

(i)

There is no
\phi
propagator.

(ii)

The interactions have a single LM field
\lambda
in the external legs.

The tree-level remains unchanged. At one-loop order, only mixed propagators appear in the internal lines, with
\phi
being the only field present in the external legs. Other configurations are forbidden, thus higher-order loop diagrams do not arise. The doubling of one-loop diagrams is attributed to the combinatorial factor
[
29
]
.

10.2
Fermionic systems

Let us consider now a fermionic system described by the complex spinor fields
\psi
and
\bar{\psi}
. The action of this system is given by

(273)

S[\psi,\bar{\psi}]=\int\mathop{dx}\mathcal{L}[\psi,\bar{\psi}].

The Euler-Lagrange equations are

(274)

\frac{\delta S}{\delta\psi}=0\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\delta S}{\delta\bar{\psi}}=0.

If the generating functional of this system is given by

(275)

Z[\eta,\bar{\eta}]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\psi}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\psi}\exp{i\int\mathop{dx}\left(\mathcal{L}[\psi,\bar{\psi}]+\eta\bar{\psi}+\bar{\eta}\psi\right)},

then, in the framework of the LM theory, it would become

(276)

\begin{split}\mathsf{Z}_{\text{LM}}[\eta,\bar{\eta},\xi,\bar{\xi}]={}&\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\psi}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\psi}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\lambda}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda}\exp{i}\int\mathop{dx}\bigg(\mathcal{L}[\psi,\bar{\psi}]+\lambda\frac{\delta S}{\delta\psi}+\bar{\lambda}\frac{\delta S}{\delta\bar{\psi}}\\
&+\bar{\psi}\eta+\bar{\eta}\psi+\bar{\eta}\lambda+\bar{\lambda}\eta+\bar{\lambda}\xi+\bar{\xi}\lambda),\end{split}

where all the sources and the LM fields
\lambda
,
\bar{\lambda}
are Grassmann fields, as the fields
\psi
and
\bar{\psi}
.

We can perform a similar analysis for this generating functional as we have done for the bosonic fields. The only difference is that the determinants appear with an opposite sign. The exact form of the generating functional is

(277)

\mathsf{Z}_{\text{LM}}[\eta,\bar{\eta},\xi,\bar{\xi}]=\sum_{\tilde{\psi}}\sum_{\tilde{\bar{\psi}}}\det\left(\frac{\delta_{L}\delta_{R}S[\tilde{\psi},\tilde{\bar{\psi}}]}{\delta\psi\delta\bar{\psi}}\right)^{2}\exp{i\int\mathop{dx}\left(\mathcal{L}[\tilde{\psi},\tilde{\bar{\psi}}]+\tilde{\bar{\psi}}\eta+\bar{\eta}\tilde{\psi}\right)}.

The determinant in Eq. (
277
) comes from

(278)

\begin{vmatrix}\dfrac{\updelta^{2}S[\psi,\bar{\psi}]}{\delta\psi\delta\psi}&\dfrac{\updelta^{2}S[\psi,\bar{\psi}]}{\delta\psi\delta\bar{\psi}}\\
\dfrac{\updelta^{2}S[\psi,\bar{\psi}]}{\delta\bar{\psi}\delta\psi}&\dfrac{\updelta^{2}S[\psi,\bar{\psi}]}{\delta\bar{\psi}\delta\bar{\psi}}\end{vmatrix}=\begin{vmatrix}0&\dfrac{\updelta^{2}S[\psi,\bar{\psi}]}{\delta\psi\delta\bar{\psi}}\\
\dfrac{\updelta^{2}S[\psi,\bar{\psi}]}{\delta\bar{\psi}\delta\psi}&0\end{vmatrix},

where
\updelta^{2}\equiv\delta_{L}\delta_{R}
. The determinant in Eq. (
278
) is the square of the usual fermionic one-loop contribution.

11
Gauge theories

The LM formalism for gauge theories requires more attention, since, in this context, the LM field turns out to be a gauge field itself. Hence, the FP procedure must be altered to accommodate the new gauge invariances.

Let us assume that
S[\phi]
is invariant under the gauge transformations

(279)

\delta\phi_{i}=H_{ij}\zeta_{j},

then the LM action is invariant under

(280)

\delta\phi_{i}=H_{ij}\zeta_{j}\quad\text{and}\quad\delta\lambda_{i}=H_{ij,l}\lambda_{l}\zeta_{j}.

We remind the reader that the comma represents the partial derivative.
In addition, we have a novel gauge invariance due to the LM field:

(281)

\delta\lambda_{i}=H_{ij}\zeta_{j}.

The variation of the action
S[\phi]
, under Eq. (
279
), is

(282)

\delta S=\int\mathop{dx}\frac{\delta S}{\delta\phi}\delta\phi=0.

When supplemented by LM fields, it becomes

(283)

\delta S_{\text{LM}}=\int\mathop{dx}\left(\frac{\delta S}{\delta\phi}\delta\phi+\delta\lambda\frac{\delta S}{\delta\phi}+\frac{\delta^{2}S}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\lambda\delta\phi\right).

Clearly, Eq. (
281
) is valid, when
\delta\phi=0
. Now, by differentiating Eq. (
282
) we obtain

(284)

\int\mathop{dx}\left(\frac{\delta^{2}S}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\delta\phi+\frac{\delta S}{\delta\phi}\frac{\delta\delta\phi}{\delta\phi}\right)=0.

Thus, as long
\delta\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{l}\delta(\delta\phi_{i})/\delta\phi_{l}=\lambda_{l}H_{ij,l}\zeta_{l}
, Eq. (
283
) is invariant under Eq. (
279
). This shows the consistency of Eq. (
280
).

The standard method is to account for these gauge invariances and build up the appropriate FP procedure, we refer the reader to Refs.
[
27
,
29
,
30
]
.

11.1
Alternative method

Alternatively, one can introduce the LM fields after quantizing the physical field
\phi
. We show that this method yields the same generating functional by applying it directly to the HP generating functional (
158
). In this method, we also need to introduce LM fields for the FP ghost fields, which are anticommuting.
This shows that the standard LM formalism and the FP procedure commute. In Chapter
7
, we provide a general demonstration.

We also show that the LM formalism is consistent with the quantum equivalence between the FOGR and the SOGR formulation previously examined. We shall obtain the SOGR formulation through the FOGR formulation.

Let us supplement the generating functional in Eq. (
158
) with the LM fields
\lambda_{\mu\nu}
and
\Lambda_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}
. The bosonic LM action is

(285)

\begin{split}S_{b\text{LM}}={}&\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}}\int\mathop{dx}\left[\mathchoice{\Lambda^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi\tau}}}}{\Lambda^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi\tau}}}}{\Lambda^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi\tau}}}}{\Lambda^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi\tau}}}}\left(\mathchoice{M^{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt}}}{M^{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt}}}{M^{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt}}}{M^{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt}}}(h)G^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}-\kappa\phi^{\pi\tau}{}_{,\rho}\right)\right]\\
&+\int\mathop{dx}\bigg[\frac{1}{\kappa}\lambda^{\alpha\beta}\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathchoice{G^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{G^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{G^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{G^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}\mathchoice{G^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi\tau}}}}{G^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi\tau}}}}{G^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi\tau}}}}{G^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi\tau}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt{\alpha\beta}}}}{\mathcal{M}^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt{\alpha\beta}}}}{\mathcal{M}^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt{\alpha\beta}}}}{\mathcal{M}^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt{\alpha\beta}}}}+\mathchoice{G^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[18.10588pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.10588pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.12555pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.23253pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta,\lambda}}}}{G^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[18.10588pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.10588pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.12555pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.23253pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta,\lambda}}}}{G^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[18.10588pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.10588pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.12555pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.23253pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta,\lambda}}}}{G^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[18.10588pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.10588pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.12555pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.23253pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta,\lambda}}}}\right)-\frac{1}{\alpha}(\partial_{\mu}\phi^{\mu\nu})\eta_{\nu\rho}(\partial_{\sigma}\lambda^{\sigma\rho})\\
&+\kappa\bar{d}_{\mu}\left[(\lambda^{\rho\sigma}_{,\rho})\partial_{\sigma}\eta^{\mu\nu}-(\lambda^{\rho\mu}_{,\rho})\partial^{\nu}+\lambda^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}\eta^{\mu\nu}-(\partial_{\rho}\partial^{\nu}\lambda^{\rho\mu})\right]d_{\nu}\bigg].\end{split}

The LM fields
c_{\mu}
and
\bar{c}_{\mu}
associated with the FP ghosts
d_{\mu}
and
\bar{d}_{\mu}
leads to the fermionic LM action:

(286)

\begin{split}S_{f\text{LM}}={}&\int\mathop{dx}\kappa\big\{\!\left[\bar{c}_{\mu}(\phi^{\rho\sigma}_{,\rho})\partial_{\sigma}\eta^{\mu\nu}-(\phi^{\rho\mu}_{,\rho})\partial^{\nu}+\phi^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}\eta^{\mu\nu}-(\partial_{\rho}\partial^{\nu}\phi^{\rho\mu})\right]d_{\nu}\\
&+\bar{d}_{\mu}\left[(\phi^{\rho\sigma}_{,\rho})\partial_{\sigma}\eta^{\mu\nu}-(\phi^{\rho\mu}_{,\rho})\partial^{\nu}+\phi^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}\eta^{\mu\nu}-(\partial_{\rho}\partial^{\nu}\phi^{\rho\mu})\right]c_{\nu}\big\}.\end{split}

The generating functional of the FOGR theory in the framework of the LM formalism is then given by

(287)

\begin{split}\mathsf{Z}_{\text{HP}}[j,J,k,K]=\int&\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi^{\mu\nu}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}G^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda^{\mu\nu}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\Lambda^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{d}_{\mu}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}d_{\nu}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{e}_{\mu}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}e_{\nu}}|\det\mathchoice{M^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}}{M^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}}{M^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}}{M^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}}(h)|\\
&\quad\times\exp{i\left(S_{\text{eff}}^{\text{I}}+S_{b\text{LM}}+S_{f\text{LM}}+S_{\text{sc}}[j,J,k,K]\right)},\end{split}

where
S_{\text{eff}}^{\text{I}}
is the effective action of the FOGR theory and
S_{\text{sc}}[j,J,k,K]
denotes the source action:

(288)

S_{\text{sc}}[j,J,k,K]=j_{\mu\nu}\phi^{\mu\nu}+j_{\mu\nu}\lambda^{\mu\nu}+J_{\lambda}^{\mu\nu}G^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}+J_{\lambda}^{\mu\nu}\Lambda^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}+k_{\mu\nu}\lambda^{\mu\nu}+K_{\lambda}^{\mu\nu}\Lambda^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}.

The Senjanović determinant in the LM formalism is the square of the usual Senjanović determinant that arises when the LM fields are absent. The LM fields duplicates the constraints of a constrained system, which leads to the square of the FP and the FS determinant.

The generating functional in Eq. (
287
) is the same obtained in
[
30
]
. Thus, the alternative method may be used to obtain the generating functional of gauge theories without requiring changing the FP procedure. Moreover, it confirms that the anticommuting fields
c_{\mu}
and
\bar{c}_{\mu}
can be interpreted as LM fields of the FP ghosts. In
[
30
]
, these fields are the FP ghosts associated with the LM field
\lambda^{\mu\nu}
. Therefore, we see a duality: the fields
c_{\mu}
,
\bar{c}_{\mu}
are the FP ghosts associated with the LM field
\lambda^{\mu\nu}
, or alternatively, the LM fields of the FP ghosts
d_{\mu}
,
\bar{d}_{\mu}
are the fields
c_{\mu}
,
\bar{c}_{\mu}
. This is shown in Fig.
11
.

Figure 11:
In the standard method (S), the introduction of the LM field
\lambda
is followed by the FP procedure. In the alternative method (A), we start with the FP procedure and then we introduce the LM fields
\lambda^{\mu\nu}
and
c_{\mu}
,
\bar{c}_{\mu}
. The order is represented by the number of solid lines, the dashed lines indicate the physical interpretation.

Now, we can proceed to obtain the SOGR theory from Eq. (
287
). We integrate the LM field
\Lambda^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}
associated with the auxiliary field
G^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}
yielding

(289)

\mathop{\delta}\left(\mathchoice{M^{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt}}}{M^{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt}}}{M^{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt}}}{M^{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt}}}(h)G^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}-\kappa\phi^{\pi\tau}{}_{,\rho}\right)=\left(\det\mathchoice{M^{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt}}}{M^{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt}}}{M^{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt}}}{M^{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt}}}\right)^{-1}\mathop{\delta}(G^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}-\mathbb{G}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}),

where
\mathbb{G}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}
is the classical value of the auxiliary field defined in Eq. (
141a
)). The determinant in Eq. (
289
) cancels the square of the Senjanović determinant in Eq. (
287
). Integrating the auxiliary field using the
\delta
-function allows us to replace the auxiliary field with its classical value. This results in

(290)

\begin{split}&\mathsf{Z}_{\text{HE}}[j,J,k,K]\\
={}&\int\mathcal{D}{\phi}_{\mu\nu}\mathcal{D}{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}\exp\bigg[i\int\mathop{dx}\bigg(-\frac{1}{2\kappa^{2}}\mathbb{G}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}[h,K]\mathchoice{M^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\sigma}\kern 3.73024pt}}}{M^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\sigma}\kern 3.73024pt}}}{M^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\sigma}\kern 3.48792pt}}}{M^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\sigma}\kern 3.48792pt}}}(\chi)(\mathbb{G}[h,K]-2\mathbb{G}[\chi,J]){}^{\sigma}_{\pi\tau}\\
&-\frac{1}{2\alpha}\left[(\partial_{\mu}\phi^{\mu\nu})^{2}+2(\partial_{\mu}\phi^{\mu\nu})\eta_{\nu\rho}(\partial_{\sigma}\lambda^{\sigma\rho})\right]+j_{\mu\nu}\phi^{\mu\nu}+k_{\mu\nu}\lambda^{\mu\nu}\bigg)\bigg]{\Xi}_{\text{FP}}(\phi,\lambda),\end{split}

where
\chi^{\mu\nu}=\eta^{\mu\nu}+\kappa(\phi^{\mu\nu}+\lambda^{\mu\nu})
. The FP sector is kept unaltered and is denoted by
\Xi_{\text{FP}}(\phi,\lambda)
. Setting
J=K=0
in Eq. (
290
) leads to the SOGR generating functional in the framework of the LM formalism
[
30
]
.

Both Eq. (
287
) and Eq. (
290
) leads to solvable, renormalizable gravity theories
[
30
]
. The perturbative expansion is truncated at one-loop order. The tree diagrams are kept unaltered, thus GR is obtained in the classical limit. The one-loop diagrams result in twice the usual contribution, which arises in the theory without LM fields.

12
Instabilities in the standard Lagrange multiplier theory

In general, the classical LM theory is given by a constrained system described by a Lagrangian
L(x,\dot{x})
(and action
S[x]
) with the Euler-Lagrange equation

(291)

\alpha_{0}\equiv\frac{\delta S[x]}{\delta x}=\frac{d}{dt}\left(\frac{\partial L}{\partial\dot{x}}\right)-\frac{\partial L}{\partial x}=0

as the constraint. Let us consider a general, standard, one-dimensional Lagrangian

(292)

L(x,\dot{x})=\frac{\dot{x}^{2}}{2}-V(x),

where
V(x)
denotes the potential, and dots denote times derivatives. The constraint is

(293)

\alpha_{0}=\ddot{x}+\partial_{x}V(x)=0,

which is the equation of motion obtained by substituting Eq. (
292
) in Eq. (
291
). The constraint
\alpha_{0}
is highly non-trivial since it is acceleration-dependent. It can be recast as the set of non-holonomic constraints:

(294)

\dot{Q}+\partial_{x}V(x)=0\quad\text{and}\quad Q-\dot{x}=0,

where we introduced a new variable
Q
. Thus, the price that we have to pay is the doubling of the degrees of freedom. It clarifies the origin of the doubling of degrees of freedom in the LM theory, which is directly related to the presence of instabilities
[
37
]
.

The LM theory is then given by the non-holonomic system described by the Lagrangian
L(x,Q)
with the non-holonomic constraints (
294
). There are two methods to approach non-holonomic systems: the standard non-holonomic mechanics based on the D’Alembert-Lagrange principle (see the reviews
[
85
,
86
]
) and vakonomic mechanics
11
11

11

It stands for mechanics of variational axiomatic kind.

[
87
]
. Vakonomic mechanics is based on the generalization of Hamilton’s principle of least action for non-holonomic systems. By following the vakonomic approach, we obtain the standard LM theory. In this approach, the extended Lagrangian

(295)

L_{\text{LM}}(x,\dot{x};y)=L(x,\dot{x})+y\left(\ddot{x}+\partial_{x}V(x)\right)

describes the dynamics of the LM theory.
Despite the apparent higher derivative nature of the Lagrangian, the Ostrogradsky theorem
[
33
,
88
]
cannot be applied due to its degeneracy. However, the resulting Hamiltonian still exhibits Ostrogradsky instabilities, which can be remedied by removing the higher derivative terms using a total derivative resulting in
12
12

12

In this approach, the LM field
\lambda
becomes dynamical.

(296)

L_{\text{LM}}(x,\dot{x};y)=L(x,\dot{x})-\dot{y}\dot{x}+y\partial_{x}V(x).

This shows that the LM theory is not a higher derivative theory in this approach.
From Eq. (
296
), we can obtain the Hamiltonian

(297)

H=-p_{x}p_{y}-\frac{p_{y}^{2}}{2}+V(x)-y\partial_{x}V(x),

where
p_{x}
and
p_{y}
are the canonical momenta of
x
and
y
. The Hamiltonian depends linearly on the momenta revealing the presence of Ostrogradsky-like instabilities in the standard LM theory. The Ostrogradsky instability leads to an unbound Hamiltonian as we can see in Eq. (
297
), and therefore the quantum standard LM theory must be plagued by Ostrogradsky-like ghosts.

One may try to reduce the phase space to remove the unphysical degrees of freedom and obtain a bounded Hamiltonian, which in our case could be done using a physical sector such as
(x,p_{y})
. Thus, the quantization of the Hamiltonian (
297
) would lead to any quantum correction since
x
and
p_{y}
commutes. This approach removes the Ostrogradsky-like instabilities, but the resulting theory is trivial. It would be simply the classical theory stated within the framework of quantum theory (known as the KvNS formalism
[
89
,
90
,
36
]
).

Therefore, it seems that the quantum LM theory would not be consistent. However, it is important to remark that vakonomic mechanics is not equivalent to non-holonomic mechanics. In fact, the vakonomic mechanics generally does not lead to the same equations of motion obtained with Newtonian mechanics
[
91
,
92
,
93
,
94
]
. Moreover, experimental results support the non-holonomic mechanics
[
95
]
.

This suggests that the standard path integral quantization of the LM theory must be modified, as the standard approach may be inappropriate for describing an LM non-holonomic system, even at the classical level. Furthermore, the quantization of non-holonomic systems within non-holonomic mechanics cannot be done systematically
[
96
]
. In principle, these systems are neither Hamiltonian nor variational. Although, in some cases, a Hamiltonian can be obtained and standard quantization procedures can be used
[
96
,
97
]
. Thus, to conclude, the consistency of the quantization of the standard LM theory must be examined carefully. This is considered in the next chapter.

In the next chapter, we propose a modification of the LM formalism in which the doubling of one-loop contributions is absent. In addition, the spurious degrees of freedom due to LM fields are also removed.

Chapter 6
Modified Lagrange multiplier formalism

In this chapter, we present a modification of the standard LM formalism, which was proposed in Ref.
[
37
]
.
We demonstrate that we can remove the doubling of degrees of freedom and the consequent additional one-loop contributions, by restoring the field redefinition invariance of the LM theory’s path integral. This proposal resolves the known drawbacks of the standard LM formalism while maintaining the renormalizability and unitarity of the resulting theory.

13
Field redefinitions

An expected propriety of any quantum path integral is the invariance under field redefinitions. Quantum fields are not observable quantities and can be redefined without affecting the physical quantities such as the
S
-matrix. In particular, for the
S
-matrix, this is known as the equivalence theorem
[
98
]
.

Field redefinitions in the quantum path integral are analogous to a change of variables of an ordinary integral. We will show this by using the general generating functional in Eq. (
262
). Under the field redefinition

(298)

\phi\to\phi^{\prime}=F[\phi]

the generating functional (
262
) transforms as

(299)

Z[j]\to Z^{\prime}[j]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi^{\prime}}\det{}\mathbb{J}_{\phi}\exp{i\int\mathop{dx}\left(\mathcal{L}(F^{-1}[\phi^{\prime}])+jF^{-1}[\phi^{\prime}]\right)},

where

(300)

\det{}\mathbb{J}_{\phi}\equiv\frac{\delta\phi}{\delta\phi^{\prime}}

denotes the Jacobian determinant associated with the field redefinition in Eq. (
298
). Note that, the source term is modified. In principle, it is equal to
jF^{-1}[\phi^{\prime}]
. However, it is necessary so that the transformed generating functional in Eq. (
299
) yields the same Green functions, that is,

(301)

i^{n}\langle 0|T\phi(x_{1})\cdots\phi(x_{n})|0\rangle=\frac{\delta^{n}Z[j]}{\delta j(x_{1})\cdots\delta j(x_{n})}=\frac{\delta^{n}Z^{\prime}[j]}{\delta j(x_{1})\cdots\delta j(x_{n})}.

In the LM formalism, any field redefinition of the physical field
\phi
must be accompanied by a corresponding field redefinition of its associated LM field
\lambda

[
37
]
. This can be understood as follows: the LM field
\lambda
enforces the equations of motion, which change under the field redefinition of
\phi
. Therefore, the invariance of the form of the LM action
13
13

13

We define
S[F^{-1}[\phi^{\prime}]]=S^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}]
.

(302)

S[\phi]+\lambda\frac{\delta S[\phi]}{\delta\phi}\to S^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}]+\lambda^{\prime}\frac{\delta S^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}]}{\delta\phi^{\prime}}

is maintained as long

(303)

\lambda^{\prime}=\lambda\frac{\delta\phi^{\prime}}{\delta\phi}.

Consequently, the generating functional (
262
) in the framework of the LM formalism (in Eq. (
264
)) transforms as

(304)

\mathsf{Z}_{\text{LM}}[j,k]\to{}\mathsf{Z}_{\text{LM}}^{\prime}[j,k],

where

(305)

\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}_{\text{LM}}[j,k]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda^{\prime}}\det{}\mathbb{J}_{\phi}^{2}\exp{i\int\mathop{dx}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\phi^{\prime})+\lambda^{\prime}\frac{\delta S^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}]}{\delta\phi^{\prime}}+jF^{-1}[\phi^{\prime}]+j\lambda^{\prime}+k\lambda^{\prime}\right)}.

Therefore, we see that the behavior of the generating functional of the standard LM theory under field redefinitions differs from the standard (see Eq. (
299
)).

Note that there is no doubling of the Jacobian determinant factor for field redefinitions of
\lambda
. This reveals an inconsistency arising from the lack of field redefinition invariance in the path integral within the framework of the standard LM formalism. In
[
37
]
, we have shown that this issue can be resolved by introducing ghost fields. Furthermore, these ghost fields are responsible for canceling the extra one-loop contributions due to the LM field
\lambda
. In the next section, we present the
modified LM formalism
in more detail.

14
Field redefinition invariant Lagrange multiplier formalism

In this section, we present the field redefinition invariant LM formalism
[
37
]
. It is a modification of the standard LM formalism studied in chapter
6
. In this section, we restrict our presentation to non-singular theories.

We proposed in
[
37
]
to introduce

(306)

\mathit{\Delta}[\phi]=\det\left(\frac{\delta^{2}S[\phi]}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\right)^{1/2}

in the measure of Eq. (
264
). Now the measure of integration of the path integral in the LM formalism transforms as

(307)

\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda}\mathit{\Delta}[\phi]\to\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda^{\prime}}\mathit{\Delta}^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}]\det{}\mathbb{J}_{\phi}^{2}=\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda^{\prime}}\mathit{\Delta}[\phi^{\prime}]\det{}\mathbb{J}_{\phi},

which is the expected behavior of the path integral under field redefinitions (cf. Eq. (
299
)). Thus, the field redefinition invariance of the path integral in the framework of the LM formalism is restored.

The determinant
\mathit{\Delta}
is the pfaffian of the Hessian of the action. We assume that the Hessian is always positive, since, in principle, an absolute value should appear in Eq. (
306
). Interestingly, this determinant only satisfies the transformation law

(308)

\mathit{\Delta}[\phi]\to\mathit{\Delta}^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}]=\det{}\mathbb{J}_{\phi}\mathit{\Delta}[\phi^{\prime}]

in the measure of integration of the path integral of the LM formalism. The determinant in Eq. (
306
) transform as
[
37
]

(309)

\mathit{\Delta}[\phi]\to\mathit{\Delta}^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}]=\det{}\mathbb{J}_{\phi}\Delta[\phi^{\prime}]\det K[\phi]

under the field redefinition (
298
), where

(310)

K[\phi]=1-\left(\frac{\delta^{2}S^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}]}{\delta\phi^{\prime}\delta\phi^{\prime}}\right)^{-1}\frac{\delta{}\mathbb{J}_{\phi}}{\delta\phi^{\prime}}{}\mathbb{J}_{\phi}^{-1}\frac{\delta S^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}]}{\delta\phi^{\prime}}.

Note that, the second term on the right-hand side of the above expression is proportional to the equations of motion. In the LM formalism, they are imposed to vanish. Hence,
K[\phi]=1
, and can be discarded.

Thus, the field redefinition invariant path integral of the LM formalism is

(311)

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{LM}}[0]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda}\det\left(\frac{\delta^{2}S[\phi]}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\right)^{+1/2}\exp iS_{\text{LM}}[\phi],

where

(312)

S_{\text{LM}}[\phi]=\int\mathop{dx}\left(\mathcal{L}(\phi)+\lambda\frac{\delta S[\phi]}{\delta\phi}\right)

is the action of the standard LM theory.

The determinant in Eq. (
311
) can be cast in local form through the introduction of ghost fields. First, we rewrite it as

(313)

\begin{split}\det\left(\frac{\delta^{2}S[\phi]}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\right)^{+1/2}=\det\left(\frac{\delta^{2}S[\phi]}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\right)\det\left(\frac{\delta^{2}S[\phi]}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\right)^{-1/2}.\end{split}

Next, we proceed by exponentiating the determinants in Eq. (
313
), which results in

(314)

\mathit{\Delta}[\phi]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}{\bar{\theta}}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\theta}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi}\exp{i}\int\mathop{dx}\left(\bar{\theta}\frac{\delta^{2}S[\phi]}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\theta+\frac{1}{2}\chi\frac{\delta^{2}S[\phi]}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\chi\right),

where
\bar{\theta},\theta
and
\chi
are scalar fermionic and bosonic fields, respectively.

It is worth mentioning the similarity with the Lee-Yang ghosts
[
99
,
100
]
, which are necessary for preserving general covariance of the path integral in the worldline formalism. In this formalism, the determinant
\sqrt{\det g^{\mu\nu}}
introduced in the measure of integration of the path integral leads to the Lee-Yang ghosts. It is analogous to the determinant considered in Eq. (
306
).

From Eq. (
314
), we obtain the ghost action

(315)

S_{\text{gh}}[\phi]=\int\mathop{dx}\left(\bar{\theta}\frac{\delta^{2}S[\phi]}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\theta+\frac{1}{2}\chi\frac{\delta^{2}S[\phi]}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\chi\right).

Then, we can proceed and define

(316)

\begin{split}S_{\text{eff}}[\phi]&=S_{\text{LM}}[\phi]+S_{\text{gh}}[\phi]\\
&=\int\mathop{dx}\left(\mathcal{L}(\phi)+\lambda\frac{\delta S[\phi]}{\delta\phi}+\bar{\theta}\frac{\delta^{2}S[\phi]}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\theta+\frac{1}{2}\chi\frac{\delta^{2}S[\phi]}{\delta\phi\delta\phi}\chi\right),\end{split}

which can be interpreted as an effective action of the modified LM formalism.
Replacing Eqs. (
313
)—(
316
) into Eq. (
311
) yields

(317)

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{LM}}[0]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\theta}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\theta}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi}\exp{i}S_{\text{eff}}[\phi],

which is the proper path integral for the LM theory
[
37
]
.

We have that

(318)

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{LM}}[0]\to\mathcal{Z^{\prime}}_{\text{LM}}[0]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\theta}^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\theta^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi^{\prime}}\det{}\mathbb{J}_{\phi}\exp{i}S_{\text{eff}}^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}],

which agrees with the expected behavior shown in Eqs. (
299
) and (
307
).
In Eq. (
318
), we see that all the fields associated with the physical field
\phi
change accordingly. Thus, under the field redefinition (
298
), these fields transforms as

(319a)

\displaystyle\lambda^{\prime}={}

\displaystyle\lambda\frac{\delta F[\phi]}{\delta\phi},

(319b)

\displaystyle\chi^{\prime}={}

\displaystyle\chi\frac{\delta F[\phi]}{\delta\phi},

(319c)

\displaystyle\theta^{\prime}={}

\displaystyle\theta\frac{\delta F[\phi]}{\delta\phi},

(319d)

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}^{\prime}={}

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}\frac{\delta F[\phi]}{\delta\phi};

while other independent fields remain unaltered. This is the analog of Eq. (
303
) in the modified LM formalism. The transformation of the bosonic fields leads to a Jacobian determinant factor of
\det{}\mathbb{J}_{\phi}
, and fermionic fields leads to the inverse
\det{}\mathbb{J}_{\phi}^{-1}
.

In
[
37
]
, it is noted that the effective action in Eq. (
316
) has a ghost-number symmetry. This symmetry also appears in gauge theories. We can use this similarity to count, naively, the degrees of freedom of the modified LM theory. We assume that the ghost fields
\theta
and
\bar{\theta}
, as the FP ghosts, cancel degrees of freedom.
14
14

14

Indeed, the FP ghosts cancel degrees of freedom, the unphysical degrees of freedom associated with longitudinal modes.
Let us denote the degrees of freedom of a field
I
by
N_{I}
and the total degrees of freedom of the fermionic ghost fields by
N_{\text{gh}}=N_{\theta}+N_{\bar{\theta}}
. The number of degrees of freedom associated with the modified LM sector is

(320)

N_{\lambda}-N_{\text{gh}}+N_{\chi}=0,

since
N_{\lambda}=N_{\chi}=N_{\text{gh}}/2=N_{\phi}
.
Consequently, the modified LM theory has

(321)

N_{\phi}+N_{\lambda}-N_{\text{gh}}+N_{\chi}=N_{\phi}

degrees of freedom.
15
15

15

We should remark that this result is also consistent with the free energy density at finite temperature
[
69
]
. A one-loop analysis shows transparently the relation between the free energy with the number of degrees of freedom.

Thus, the degrees of freedom in the modified LM formalism are not doubled. It is kept unaltered. Now, let us show that the doubling of the one-loop contributions is also absent.

First, we conveniently rewrite Eq. (
317
)
as

(322)

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{LM}}[0]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi}\det\left[\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\phi)\right]\det\left[\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\phi)\right]^{-1/2}\mathop{\delta}\left[\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\phi)\right]\exp{i}\int\mathop{dx}\mathcal{L}(\phi)

in which the LM field
\lambda
and the ghost fields are integrated.
Using the functional analog of the Eq. (
267
):

(323)

\delta\left(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\phi)\right)=\sum_{\tilde{\phi}}\det[\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\phi})]^{-1}\mathop{\delta}(\phi-\tilde{\phi})

in Eq. (
322
), we obtain

(324)

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{LM}}[0]=\sum_{\tilde{\phi}(x)}\det[\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\phi})]^{-1/2}\exp{i}\int\mathop{dx}\mathcal{L}(\tilde{\phi}).

This is the exact generating functional of the modified LM theory, as shown in Eq. (
269
). The exponential factor is similar, but the determinant is no longer squared compared with the usual one-loop determinant.

The field redefinition invariant formulation of the LM formalism leads to the same tree-level and one-loop contributions obtained with the generating functional in Eq. (
262
). However, the higher-loop order contributions vanish. Remarkably, the modification proposed in
[
37
]
establishes a formalism in which the radiative effects are restricted to one-loop order, while the doubling of degrees of freedom and one-loop contributions are absent.

14.1
Diagrammatic analysis

Here, we present a diagrammatic analysis of the modified LM formalism. For an analysis in more detail, we refer to
[
37
]
.

Substituting Eq. (
270
) in Eq. (
315
) yields

(325)

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\text{gh}}(\phi)={}&\bar{\theta}_{i}\left(a^{(2)}_{ij}+a^{(3)}_{ijk}\phi_{k}+\frac{1}{2!}a^{(4)}_{ijkl}\phi_{k}\phi_{l}+\cdots\right)\theta_{j}\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\chi_{i}\left(a^{(2)}_{ij}+a^{(3)}_{ijk}\phi_{k}+\frac{1}{2!}a^{(4)}_{ijkl}\phi_{k}\phi_{l}+\cdots\right)\chi_{j}.\end{split}

This is the Lagrangian of the ghost fields. We see that the propagators of the ghosts are equal to
a_{ij}
, which is equal to the mixed propagators
\left\langle\phi_{i}\lambda_{j}\right\rangle
. The ghost sector is independent of the LM field
\lambda
. Now we can examine how the ghost fields will affect perturbatively the LM formalism.

The perturbation theory at the lowest order, tree-level, is kept unaltered since ghosts can only appear in closed loops. This is a characteristic shared by most known ghost fields, such as the FP, Senjanović, and Lee-Yang ghosts. The ghost fields do not interact with the LM field
\lambda
. Thus, their contributions only affect the Green’s function with
\phi
in the external legs. Moreover, since the propagator
\phi
vanishes, we cannot have any ghost diagram of order higher than one-loop. Therefore, the ghost fields contribute only at one-loop order.

In fig.
12
, we present some of the ghost contributions coming from Eq. (
325
).

Figure 12:
Ghost contributions to 4-point amplitude
(\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\phi_{k}\phi_{l})
in the modified LM theory. Pointed and dashed lines represent respectively the ghost fields
\bar{\theta}
,
\theta
, and
\chi
.

The fermionic loops in Fig.
12
(a) lead to a negative sign compared with the bosonic loops in Fig.
12
(b). In addition, the combinatorial factor is also twice. The resulting contribution is exactly the required to remove the additional one-loop contributions coming from the LM field
\lambda
(see Fig.
13
).

Figure 13:
One-loop contribution in the modified LM theory to 4-point amplitude
(\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\phi_{k}\phi_{l})
. Ghost contributions are responsible for canceling the extra contributions due to the LM field. The relative factors are indicated.

The results in this section are more transparent in the diagonalized form of the modified LM action (which is realized by the shift
\phi\to\varphi-\lambda
). We have shown in
[
30
]
that diagrams of order higher than one-loop can be constructed in the diagonal LM theory, but the resulting contribution vanishes.

In Fig.
14
we compare the one-loop diagrams from the original theory described by the action
S[\phi]
, modified LM theory, and the diagonalized LM theory. Inside each loop, we have the respective overall factor that must be compared with the one-loop diagram from the original theory. In each row, the sum of the overall factors yields
1/2
, including the diagonalized modified LM theory agreeing with the previous diagrammatic analysis presented in section
14
. In Fig.
14
(c) (diagonalized modified LM theory) we see that the ghost contributions are responsible for canceling the extra contribution coming from the LM field
\lambda
.

Figure 14:
One-loop contributions to the two-point amplitudes
(\phi_{i}\phi_{j})
. Diagram (a) from the original theory, diagrams (b) of the modified LM theory, and diagrams (c) of the diagonalized formulation. The relative factor between the diagrams is indicated. In the diagonalized modified LM theory the cancellation between the LM field and ghost contributions is evident.

In the modified LM theory the quantum corrections are restricted to the one-loop order. While in the diagonalized formulation loop diagrams of higher order appear which, for consistency, must add to zero.
In
[
30
]
we checked the consistency of the diagonalized standard LM theory using a scalar
\phi^{3}
model showing that all two-loop order contributions vanish. The same analysis is applied to the ghost contributions of the diagonalized modified LM theory. Together with the analysis found in
[
30
]
the consistency check for the diagonalized formulation of the modified LM theory is completed.
In Figs.
15
and
16
, we analyze two specific topologies of two-loop order diagrams from the scalar
\phi^{3}
model. It is shown that the total contribution vanishes, in particular the contribution coming from ghost diagrams. In the diagonalized modified LM theory ghosts also interact with the LM field
\lambda
which is essential for the consistency of the diagonalized modified LM theory.

Figure 15:
The 1PI two-loop diagrams (a, p) with ghost-loops in the modified diagonal LM theory. The sum of the diagrams (a) from the standard diagonal LM theory vanishes, while ghost diagrams from the modified formalism cancel within each pair (p). The pairs (p1, p2) cancel by the oppositive sign of internal propagators of
\phi
and
\lambda
, while the pairs (p3, p4) canceling due to the extra minus sign of the
(\lambda\lambda\phi)
vertex
[
30
]
. We indicate the relative factor between the diagram.

Figure 16:
Reducible two-loop order diagrams with ghost-loops in the modified diagonal LM theory. The sum of the diagrams vanishes in each column. Relative factors are indicated.

15
Additional symmetries

In this short section, we present the symmetries of the modified LM theory. We also comment on the Slavnov-Taylor-like identities obtained from the BRST-like symmetry.

The effective action of the modified LM theory in Eq. (
316
) is invariant under

(326)

\delta\bar{\theta}=-\sigma\bar{\theta},\quad\delta\theta=\sigma\theta,\quad\delta\phi=\delta\lambda=\delta\chi=0;

where
\sigma
is a commuting parameter.
This symmetry is related to the conservation of Lee-Yang-like
ghost charge

Q_{\text{gh}}
, which is directly related to the
ghost-number

[
37
]
. It can be shown that
Q_{\text{gh}}\theta=\sigma
,
Q_{\text{gh}}\bar{\theta}=-\sigma
while
Q_{\text{gh}}\phi=Q_{\text{gh}}\lambda=Q_{\text{gh}}\chi=0
.

In addition, the modified LM theory is also invariant under the
BRST-like
symmetry

(327)

\delta\theta=\chi\eta,\quad\delta\chi=\eta\bar{\theta},\quad\delta\phi=\delta\lambda=\delta\bar{\theta}=0,

and the anti-BRST symmetry

(328)

\bar{\delta}\bar{\theta}=\chi\bar{\eta},\quad\bar{\delta}\chi=\theta\bar{\eta},\quad\bar{\delta}\phi=-\bar{\delta}\lambda=\bar{\delta}\theta=0,

where (
\bar{\eta}
)
\eta
is an anticommuting parameter of the (anti)BRST symmetry.

15.1
Slavnov-Taylor-Like identities

The master equations derived from the (anti)BRST-like symmetry are simply given by

(329a)

\displaystyle\int\mathop{dx}\left(\chi\frac{\delta\Gamma_{\text{mLM}}}{\delta\theta}+\bar{\theta}\frac{\delta\Gamma_{\text{mLM}}}{\delta\chi}\right)={}

\displaystyle 0,

(329b)

\displaystyle\int\mathop{dx}\left(\chi\frac{\delta\Gamma_{\text{mLM}}}{\delta\bar{\theta}}-{\theta}\frac{\delta\Gamma_{\text{mLM}}}{\delta\chi}\right)={}

\displaystyle 0,

where
\Gamma_{\text{mLM}}
is the effective action of the modified LM formalism.

We can now obtain the Slavnov-Taylor-like identities involving the 1PI Green functions. For instance, taking the functional derivative
\delta^{2}/\delta\chi\delta\bar{\theta}
of Eq. (
329a
) gives

(330)

\frac{\delta^{2}\Gamma_{\text{mLM}}}{\delta\bar{\theta}\delta\theta}+\frac{\delta^{2}\Gamma_{\text{mLM}}}{\delta\chi\delta\chi}=0.

Eq. (
330
) is clearly satisfied in all orders.
We also find other identities, such as:

(331)

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{2}\Gamma_{\text{mLM}}}{\delta\phi\delta\bar{\theta}}={}

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{2}\Gamma_{\text{mLM}}}{\delta\phi\delta{\theta}}=0,

(332)

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{2}\Gamma_{\text{mLM}}}{\delta\chi\delta\chi\delta\chi}={}

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{2}\Gamma_{\text{mLM}}}{\delta\chi\cdots\delta\chi\delta\chi}=0.

The Slavnov-Taylor-Like identities implies that

(333)
