\Gamma_{\text{mLM}}=\Gamma_{\text{sLM}}+\left(\frac{1}{2}\chi^{2}+\bar{\theta}\theta\right)\frac{\delta^{2}S_{0}}{\delta\phi\delta\phi},

where

\Gamma_{\text{sLM}}
is the effective action of the standard LM formalism.

Chapter 7
Modified Lagrange multiplier formalism with gauge symmetries

In this chapter, we consider the case of a gauge theory in the framework of the modified LM formalism. We show that the Lee-Yang-like ghosts are also gauge fields, requiring careful consideration in the quantization of these theories. First, we review our results in
[
101
]
, where we extended the FP procedure to accommodate the additional invariances introduced by the LM field
\lambda
and the associated ghosts
\chi
,
\bar{\theta}
and
\theta
. We also present the corresponding BRST transformations.

16
Gauge invariances

The classical action

(334)

S_{0}=\int\mathop{dx}\mathcal{L}_{0}(\phi),

which is invariant under the gauge transformation

(335)

\delta\phi_{i}=\phi^{\prime}_{i}-\phi_{i}=H_{ij}(\phi)\xi_{j},

in the framework of the modified LM theory is supplemented by an LM field
\lambda_{i}
and the Lee-Yang ghosts
\bar{\theta}_{i}
,
\theta_{i}
,
\chi_{i}
:

(336)

S_{\text{mLM}}=S_{0}+\frac{\delta S}{\delta\phi_{i}}\lambda_{i}+\frac{\delta^{2}S}{\delta\phi_{i}\delta\phi_{j}}\left(\bar{\theta}_{i}\theta_{j}+\frac{1}{2}\chi_{i}\chi_{j}\right).

The invariance in Eq. (
335
) implies that

(337)

\delta S_{0}=\int\mathop{dx}\mathcal{L}_{0,i}H_{ij}\xi_{j}=0,

where commas denote derivatives with respect to
\phi
:
H_{I,k}\equiv\frac{\delta H_{I}}{\delta\phi_{k}}
,
which indicates that the action (
336
) is invariant under the gauge transformation:

(338)

\delta\lambda_{i}=H_{ij}(\phi_{i})\zeta_{j}.

Moreover, from Eq. (
335
), we obtain that

(339)

\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{0}}{\partial\phi_{i}}=\frac{\partial\phi_{j}^{\prime}}{\partial\phi_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{0}}{\partial\phi_{j}^{\prime}}=\left(\delta_{ji}+\frac{\partial H_{jk}}{\partial\phi_{i}}\xi_{k}\right)\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{0}}{\partial\phi_{j}^{\prime}},

and

(340)

\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{L}_{0}}{\partial\phi_{i}\partial\phi_{j}}=\frac{\partial\phi_{m}^{\prime}}{\partial\phi_{i}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi_{m}^{\prime}}\left[\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{0}}{\partial\phi_{j}^{\prime}}+\frac{\partial H_{nk}}{\partial\phi_{j}}\xi_{k}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{0}}{\partial\phi_{n}^{\prime}}\right]

that can be expanded to

(341)

\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{L}_{0}}{\partial\phi_{i}\partial\phi_{j}}=\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{L}_{0}}{\partial\phi_{i}^{\prime}\partial\phi_{j}^{\prime}}+\frac{\partial^{2}H_{lk}}{\partial\phi_{i}\partial\phi_{j}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{0}}{\partial\phi_{l}^{\prime}}\xi_{k}+\left(\frac{\partial H_{lk}}{\partial\phi_{i}}\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{L}_{0}}{\partial\phi_{j}^{\prime}\partial\phi_{l}^{\prime}}+\frac{\partial H_{lk}}{\partial\phi_{j}}\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{L}_{0}}{\partial\phi_{i}^{\prime}\partial\phi_{l}^{\prime}}\right)\xi_{k}+O(\xi^{2}).

By comparing the structure in Eq. (
341
) to the structure of the action (
336
), we see that Eq. (
335
) is accompanied with

(342a)

\displaystyle\delta\lambda_{i}={}

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\partial H_{ik}}{\partial\phi_{j}}\lambda_{j}+\frac{\partial^{2}H_{ik}}{\partial\phi_{m}\partial\phi_{n}}\left(\bar{\theta}_{m}\theta_{n}+\frac{1}{2}\chi_{m}\chi_{n}\right)\right]\xi_{k},

(342b)

\displaystyle\delta\bar{\theta}_{i}={}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial H_{ik}}{\partial\phi_{j}}\bar{\theta}_{j}\xi_{k},

(342c)

\displaystyle\delta\theta_{i}={}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial H_{ik}}{\partial\phi_{j}}\theta_{j}\xi_{k},

(342d)

\displaystyle\delta\chi_{i}={}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial H_{ik}}{\partial\phi_{j}}\chi_{j}\xi_{k}.

From Eq. (
335
), we get

(343)

(\mathcal{L}_{0,i}H_{ik})_{,j}=\mathcal{L}_{0,ij}H_{ik}+\mathcal{L}_{0,i}H_{ik,j}=0,

which implies that there are also the gauge transformations

(344a)

\displaystyle\delta\lambda_{i}={}

\displaystyle H_{ik,j}\left(\chi_{j}\sigma_{k}+\bar{\theta}_{j}\tau_{k}-\theta_{j}\pi_{k}\right),

(344b)

\displaystyle\delta\bar{\theta}_{i}={}

\displaystyle H_{ij}\pi_{j},

(344c)

\displaystyle\delta\theta_{i}={}

\displaystyle H_{ij}\tau_{j},

(344d)

\displaystyle\delta\chi_{i}={}

\displaystyle H_{ij}\sigma_{j};

where
\pi_{i}
and
\tau_{i}
are Grassmann gauge parameters.

Thus, we find out that there are five independent gauge transformations. Each invariance is associated with a field:
\phi_{i}
,
\lambda_{i}
,
\chi_{i}
,
\bar{\theta}_{i}
and
\theta_{i}
. Therefore, we see that both the LM field and the Lee-Yang ghost fields are now gauge fields.

To quantize the action (
336
), we extend the FP to accommodate these five gauge transformations. In order to use the FP, we impose the closure of the gauge algebra, that is,

(345)

[\delta_{\xi_{1}},\delta_{\xi_{2}}]\phi_{i}=\delta_{\xi_{3}}\phi_{i}\equiv f_{mn|j}H_{ij}\xi_{1}^{m}\xi_{2}^{n},

and that the condition

(346)

H_{ij,pq}=f_{mn|i,j}=0

is satisfied by
H_{ij}(\phi)
and the structure constants
f_{mn|i}
,
which implies that the original theory is a gauge theory of YM type.
16
16

16

In
[
101
]
, it is shown in Appendix C, that the closure of the algebra and the condition Eq. (
346
) is retained in the framework of the modified LM theory.

The gauge gravity theories based on the Hilbert-Einstein action or Hilbert-Palatini action that are treated here are also theories of this type. Another example of this type of theory is the gauge theory of the Poincaré group based on Einstein-Cartan action
[
102
]
. This theory describes more general spacetimes in which torsion is present.

17
Faddeev-Popov quantization

The path integral

(347)

\mathcal{Z}[0]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\theta}_{i}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\theta_{i}}}\exp{i\int\mathop{dx}\left[\mathcal{L}_{0}(\phi)+\mathcal{L}_{0,i}\lambda_{i}+\mathcal{L}_{0,ij}(\phi)\left(\bar{\theta}_{i}\theta_{j}+\frac{1}{2}\chi_{i}\chi_{j}\right)\right]}

is not well-defined, since the action in Eq. (
347
) is singular. We need to fix the gauge and introduce the FP ghost contribution. Thus, we insert the unity:

(348)

\begin{split}1={}&\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\xi_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\zeta_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\sigma_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\pi_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\tau_{i}}\delta\left\{F_{ij}\left[\begin{pmatrix}\phi_{j}\\
\lambda_{j}\\
\chi_{j}\\
\bar{\theta}_{j}\\
\theta_{j}\end{pmatrix}+M_{jp}\begin{pmatrix}\zeta_{p}\\
\xi_{p}\\
\sigma_{p}\\
\pi_{p}\\
\tau_{p}\end{pmatrix}\right]-\begin{pmatrix}p_{i}\\
q_{i}\\
r_{i}\\
s_{i}\\
t_{i}\end{pmatrix}\right\}\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}{M}_{jp},\end{split}

where

(349)

M_{jp}=\begin{pmatrix}0&H_{jp}&0&0&0\\
H_{jp}&H_{jp,k}\lambda_{k}&H_{jp,k}\chi_{k}&-H_{jp,k}\theta_{k}&H_{jp,k}{\bar{\theta}}_{k}\\
0&H_{jp,k}\chi_{k}&H_{jp}&0&0\\
0&H_{jp,k}\bar{\theta}_{k}&0&H_{jp}&0\\
0&H_{jp,k}\theta_{k}&0&0&H_{jp}\\
\end{pmatrix},

\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}{M}_{jp}
is the FP superdeterminant and
F_{ij}
is the gauge fixing condition.

Since the FP determinant is gauge invariant, we perform the gauge transformations (
335
), (
341
), (
338
), (
344
) with
(-\xi_{i},-\zeta_{i},-\sigma_{i},-\pi_{i},-\tau_{i})
and then insert the constant

(350)

\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}p_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}q_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}r_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}s_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}t_{i}}\exp i\int\mathop{dx}\left(-\frac{1}{2\alpha}p_{i}^{2}-\frac{1}{\alpha}p_{i}q_{i}-\frac{1}{2\alpha}r_{i}^{2}-\frac{1}{\alpha}s_{i}t_{i}\right)

which extends the gauge condition
F_{ij}
in a manner analogous to the
R_{\xi}
gauges.

The path integral is now independent of
(\xi_{i},\zeta_{i},\sigma_{i},\pi_{i},\tau_{i})
, the
\delta
-functions in Eq. (
348
) becomes

(351)

\delta\left[F_{ij}\begin{pmatrix}\phi_{j}\\
\lambda_{j}\\
\chi_{j}\\
\bar{\theta}_{j}\\
\theta_{j}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}p_{i}\\
q_{i}\\
r_{i}\\
s_{i}\\
t_{i}\end{pmatrix}\right]

which allows us to integrate over
(p_{i},q_{i},r_{i},s_{i},t_{i})
. This leads to

(352)

\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}{M}_{jp}\exp i\int\mathop{dx}\left(-\frac{1}{2\alpha}(F_{ij}\phi_{j})^{2}-\frac{1}{\alpha}(F_{ij}\phi_{j})(F_{ik}\lambda_{k})-\frac{1}{2\alpha}(F_{ij}\chi_{j})^{2}-\frac{1}{\alpha}(F_{ij}\bar{\theta}_{j})(F_{ik}\theta_{k})\right),

The path integral (
347
) is now given by

(353)

\mathcal{Z}[0]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\theta}_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\theta_{i}}\exp\left[i\int\mathop{dx}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\text{EFF}}+\mathcal{L}_{\text{GF}}\right)\right]\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}{M}_{jp},

where

(354)

\mathcal{L}_{\text{EFF}}=\mathcal{L}_{0}(\phi_{i})+\mathcal{L}_{0,i}\lambda_{i}+\mathcal{L}_{0,ij}(\phi_{i})\left(\bar{\theta}_{i}\theta_{j}+\frac{1}{2}\chi_{i}\chi_{j}\right),

\mathcal{L}_{\text{GF}}
is the gauge fixing Lagrangian

(355)

-\frac{1}{2\alpha}(F_{ij}\phi_{j})^{2}-\frac{1}{\alpha}(F_{ij}\phi_{j})(F_{ik}\lambda_{k})-\frac{1}{2\alpha}(F_{ij}\chi_{j})^{2}-\frac{1}{\alpha}(F_{ij}\bar{\theta}_{j})(F_{ik}\theta_{k}),

and
\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}{M}_{jp}
is the FP superdeterminant of Eq. (
348
).

The FP superdeterminant can be exponentiated using Grassmann ghost fields
(\bar{c}_{i},c_{i})
,
(\bar{d}_{i},d_{i})
,
(\bar{e}_{i},e_{i})
and complex ghost fields
(\tilde{\gamma}_{i},\gamma_{i})
,
(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i},\varepsilon_{i})

[
103
]
. Note that, we denote the complex conjugate of
A_{i}
by
\tilde{A}_{i}
. We find that

(356)

\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}{M}_{jp}={}\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{c}_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}c_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{d}_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}d_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{e}_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}e_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\tilde{\gamma}_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\gamma_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\varepsilon_{i}}\exp i\int\mathop{dx}\mathcal{L}_{\text{GH}},

where

(357)

\mathcal{L}_{\text{GH}}=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{d}_{i}\\
\bar{c}_{i}\\
\bar{e}_{i}\\
\tilde{\gamma}_{i}\\
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}\end{pmatrix}^{\mathrm{T}}F_{ij}\begin{pmatrix}0&H_{jp}&0&0&0\\
H_{jp}&H_{jp}+H_{jp,k}\lambda_{k}&H_{jp,k}\chi_{k}&-H_{jp,k}\theta_{k}&H_{jp,k}{\bar{\theta}}_{k}\\
0&H_{jp,k}\chi_{k}&H_{jp}&0&0\\
0&H_{jp,k}\bar{\theta}_{k}&0&H_{jp}&0\\
0&H_{jp,k}\theta_{k}&0&0&H_{jp}\\
\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}d_{p}\\
c_{p}\\
e_{p}\\
\gamma_{p}\\
\varepsilon_{p}\end{pmatrix},

Note that, we have used

(358)

\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}\begin{pmatrix}0&A&0&0&0\\
A&B&C&-D&E\\
0&C&A&0&0\\
0&E&0&A&0\\
0&D&0&0&A\\
\end{pmatrix}=\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}\begin{pmatrix}0&A&0&0&0\\
A&A+B&C&-D&E\\
0&C&A&0&0\\
0&E&0&A&0\\
0&D&0&0&A\\
\end{pmatrix},

which results in the following ghost action:

(359)

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\text{GH}}={}&\bar{d}_{i}F_{ij}H_{jp}c_{p}+\bar{c}_{i}F_{ij}H_{jp}d_{p}+\bar{c}_{i}F_{ij}H_{jp}c_{p}+\bar{e}_{i}F_{ij}H_{jp}e_{p}+\tilde{\gamma}_{i}F_{ij}H_{jp}\gamma_{p}+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}F_{ij}H_{jp}\varepsilon_{p}\\
&+\bar{c}_{i}F_{ij}H_{jp,k}\lambda_{k}c_{p}+\bar{c}_{i}F_{ij}H_{jp,k}\chi_{k}e_{p}-\bar{c}_{i}F_{ij}H_{jp,k}\theta_{k}\gamma_{p}+\bar{c}_{i}F_{ij}H_{jp,k}\bar{\theta}_{k}\varepsilon_{p}\\
&+\bar{e}_{i}F_{ij}H_{jp,k}\chi_{k}c_{p}+\tilde{\gamma}_{i}F_{ij}H_{jp,k}\bar{\theta_{k}}c_{p}+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}F_{ij}H_{jp,k}\theta_{k}c_{p}.\end{split}

This is required to obtain a total effective Lagrangian

(360)

\mathcal{L}_{\text{T}}=\mathcal{L}_{\text{EFF}}+\mathcal{L}_{\text{GF}}+\mathcal{L}_{\text{GH}}

that is invariant under a standard “BRST” transformation
[
38
,
39
]
.

18
BRST invariance

Using the standard procedure, one can find that the following BRST transformation

(361a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\phi_{i}={}

\displaystyle H_{ij}c_{j},

(361b)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\lambda_{i}={}

\displaystyle H_{ij}d_{j}+H_{ij,k}\lambda_{k}c_{j}+H_{ij,k}(\chi_{k}e_{j}+\bar{\theta}_{k}\varepsilon_{j}-\theta_{k}{\gamma}_{j})

(361c)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\chi_{i}={}

\displaystyle H_{ij}e_{j}+H_{ij,k}\chi_{k}c_{j},

(361d)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\bar{\theta}_{i}={}

\displaystyle H_{ij}\gamma_{j}+H_{ij,k}\bar{\theta}_{k}c_{j},

(361e)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\theta_{i}={}

\displaystyle H_{ij}\varepsilon_{j}+H_{ij,k}{\theta}_{k}c_{j},

(361f)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}c_{j}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}f_{mn|j}c_{m}c_{n},

(361g)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\gamma_{j}={}

\displaystyle-f_{mn|j}c_{m}\gamma_{n},

(361h)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\varepsilon_{j}={}

\displaystyle-f_{mn|j}c_{m}\varepsilon_{n},

(361i)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\bar{c}_{i}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\alpha}F_{ij}(\phi_{j}+\lambda_{j}),\ \mathsf{s}\bar{d}_{i}=-\frac{1}{\alpha}F_{ij}\lambda_{j},\ \mathsf{s}\bar{e}_{i}=-\frac{1}{\alpha}F_{ij}\chi_{j},\ \mathsf{s}\tilde{\gamma}_{i}=-\frac{1}{\alpha}F_{ij}{\theta}_{j},\ \mathsf{s}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}=\frac{1}{\alpha}F_{ij}\bar{\theta}_{j};

leaves the total Lagrangian (
360
) invariant
[
101
]
. By construction, the BRST transformation (
361
) is nilpotent
\mathsf{s}^{2}=0
.

19
First-order Yang-Mills theory

In the Ref.
[
101
]
, the SOYM theory is quantized in the modified LM formalism.
Here, we consider the quantization of the FOYM theory in the framework of the modified LM formalism.
Since the first-class constraints remain unaltered with the introduction of the auxiliary fields, the contributions from the FP procedure are consistent across both formulations.
This remains valid even in the presence of the LM fields from the LM theory, which shows that the modified LM formalism is consistent with these different formulations of the YM theory.

The classical FOYM Lagrangian reads

(362)

\mathfrak{L}_{0}=-\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a}\mathcal{F}^{a\,\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a}F^{a\,\mu\nu}[A_{\mu}^{a}],

where we defined

(363)

F_{\mu\nu}^{a}[B_{\mu}^{a}]=\partial_{\mu}B_{\nu}^{a}-\partial_{\nu}B_{\mu}^{a}+gf^{abc}B_{\mu}^{b}B_{\nu}^{c},

and
\mathcal{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a}
is the auxiliary field.
It is invariant under the gauge transformation

(364)

\delta A_{\mu}^{a}=D_{\mu}^{ab}(A)\xi^{b},\quad\delta F_{\mu\nu}^{a}=gf^{abc}F_{\mu\nu}^{b}\xi^{c}\quad\text{and}\quad\delta\mathcal{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a}=gf^{abc}\mathcal{F}_{\mu\nu}^{b}\xi^{c}.

We can identify
H_{ij}(\phi_{i})
with the covariant derivative, which is given by

(365)

D_{\mu}^{ab}(B)=\partial_{\mu}\delta^{ab}+gf^{apb}B_{\mu}^{b}.

We also have
f_{mn|i}\mapsto f^{abc}
(the structural constants (
2
)).

The effective action of the FOYM in the framework of the modified LM theory is given by

(366)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{L}_{\text{EFF}}={}&\mathfrak{L}_{\text{0}}+\lambda_{\mu}^{a}D^{ab}_{\nu}(A)\mathcal{F}^{b\nu\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda_{\mu\nu}^{a}(\mathcal{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a}-F_{\mu\nu}^{a})-\frac{1}{4}\chi_{\mu\nu}^{a}\chi^{a\,\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\chi_{\mu\nu}^{a}F^{a\,\mu\nu}[\chi_{\mu}^{a}]\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\theta}_{\mu\nu}^{a}\theta^{a\,\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\bar{\theta}_{\mu\nu}^{a}F^{a\,\mu\nu}[\theta_{\mu}^{a}]+\frac{1}{2}F^{a\,\mu\nu}[\bar{\theta}_{\mu}^{a}]\theta_{\mu\nu}^{a}.\end{split}

We will employ the Lorenz gauge fixing:

(367)

F_{ij}A_{j}\mapsto(\partial^{\mu}\delta^{ab})A_{\mu}^{b}=\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu}^{a}=\partial^{\mu}\lambda_{\mu}^{a}=\partial^{\mu}\chi_{\mu}^{a}=\partial^{\mu}\bar{\theta}_{\mu}^{a}=\partial^{\mu}\theta_{\mu}^{a}=0

which leaves us with the following gauge fixing Lagrangian

(368)

\mathfrak{L}_{\text{GF}}=-\frac{1}{2\alpha}(\partial\cdot A^{a})^{2}-\frac{1}{\alpha}(\partial\cdot\lambda^{a})(\partial\cdot A^{a})-\frac{1}{2\alpha}(\partial\cdot\chi^{a})^{2}-\frac{1}{\alpha}(\partial\cdot\bar{\theta}^{a})(\partial\cdot\theta^{a}),

where
\alpha
is a gauge parameter and
\partial\cdot X_{I}\equiv\partial_{\mu}X^{\mu}_{I}
(
I
are internal indices).
The corresponding ghost Lagrangian is

(369)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{L}_{\text{GH}}={}&\bar{c}^{a}\partial\cdot D^{ab}(A+\lambda)c^{b}+\bar{d}^{a}\partial\cdot D^{ab}(A)c^{b}+\bar{c}^{a}\partial\cdot D^{ab}(A)d^{b}\\
&+\bar{e}^{a}\partial\cdot D^{ab}(A)e^{b}+\tilde{\gamma}^{a}\partial\cdot D^{ab}(A)\gamma^{b}+\tilde{\varepsilon}^{a}\partial\cdot D^{ab}(A)\varepsilon^{b}\\
&+\bar{c}^{a}gf^{apb}\partial\cdot\chi^{p}e^{b}+\bar{e}^{a}gf^{apb}\partial\cdot\chi^{p}c^{b}-\bar{c}^{a}gf^{apb}\partial\cdot\theta^{p}\gamma^{b}\\
&+\tilde{\gamma}^{a}gf^{apb}\partial\cdot{\bar{\theta}}^{p}c^{b}+\bar{c}^{a}gf^{apb}\partial\cdot{\bar{\theta}}^{p}\varepsilon^{b}+\tilde{\varepsilon}^{a}gf^{apb}\partial\cdot\theta^{p}c^{b},\end{split}

which is the same of the SOYM within the framework of the modified LM formalism
[
101
]
.

The generating functional of the FOYM theory, in the modified LM theory, is given by

(370)

\begin{split}\mathcal{Z}[{}\mathbf{J},{}\bm{\mathcal{J}},{}\bm{\eta},\bar{{}\bm{\eta}}]={}&\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}{}\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}{}\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}{}\mathbf{c}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{{}\mathbf{c}}^{a}}\\
&\quad\exp i\int\mathop{dx}\left(\mathfrak{L}_{\text{EFF}}+\mathfrak{L}_{\text{GF}}+\mathfrak{L}_{\text{GH}}+\bar{{}\mathbf{J}}^{a\,\mu}{}\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{a}+{}\bm{\mathcal{J}}^{a\,\mu\nu}{}\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{a}+\bar{{}\bm{\eta}}^{a}{}\mathbf{c}^{a}+\bar{{}\mathbf{c}}^{a}{}\bm{\eta}^{a}\right),\end{split}

where we have employed the compact notation:

\displaystyle{}\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{a}=(A_{\mu}^{a},\lambda_{\mu}^{a},\chi_{\mu}^{a},\bar{\theta}_{\mu}^{a},\theta_{\mu}^{a}),

\displaystyle{}\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{a}=(\mathcal{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a},\Lambda_{\mu\nu}^{a},\chi_{\mu\nu}^{a},\bar{\theta}_{\mu\nu}^{a},\theta_{\mu\nu}^{a})

\displaystyle{}\mathbf{c}^{a}=(c^{a},d^{a},e^{a},\gamma^{a},\varepsilon^{a}),

\displaystyle{}\bm{\bar{c}}^{a}=(\bar{c}^{a},\bar{d}^{a},\bar{e}^{a},\tilde{\gamma}^{a},\tilde{\varepsilon}^{a})

and

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathcal{D}{}\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{a}}\equiv\mathop{\mathcal{D}A_{\mu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda_{\mu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi_{\mu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\theta}_{\mu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\theta_{\mu}^{a}},

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{a}}\equiv\mathop{\mathcal{D}\mathcal{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}{\Lambda}_{\mu\nu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi_{\mu\nu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\theta}_{\mu\nu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{a}},

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathcal{D}{}\mathbf{c}^{a}}\equiv\mathop{\mathcal{D}c^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}d^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}e^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\gamma^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\varepsilon^{a}},

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{{}\mathbf{c}}^{a}}\equiv\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{c}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{d}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{e}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\tilde{\gamma}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\tilde{\varepsilon}^{a}}.

The sources terms are defined as

(371a)

\displaystyle\bar{{}\mathbf{J}}^{a\,\mu}{}\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{a}\equiv{}

\displaystyle J^{a\,\mu}(A_{\mu}^{a}+\lambda_{\mu}^{a})+K^{a\,\mu}\lambda_{\mu}^{a}+L^{a\,\mu}\chi_{\mu}^{a}+\bar{\eta}^{a\,\mu}\bar{\theta}_{\mu}^{a}+\bar{\kappa}^{a\,\mu}\theta_{\mu}^{a},

(371b)

\displaystyle{}\bm{\mathcal{\bar{J}}}^{a\,\mu\nu}{}\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{a}\equiv{}

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{a\,\mu}(\mathcal{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a}+\Lambda_{\mu\nu}^{a})+\mathcal{K}^{a\,\mu\nu}\Lambda_{\mu\nu}^{a}+\mathcal{L}^{a\,\mu\nu}\chi_{\mu\nu}^{a}+\bar{\theta}_{\mu\nu}^{a}\Omega^{a\,\mu\nu}+\bar{\Omega}^{a\,\mu\nu}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{a},

(371c)

\displaystyle\bar{{}\bm{\eta}}^{a}{}\mathbf{c}^{a}\equiv{}

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}^{a}(c^{a}+d^{a})+\bar{\kappa}^{a}d^{a}+\bar{\upsilon}^{a}e^{a}+\tilde{J}^{a}\gamma^{a}+\tilde{K}^{a}\varepsilon^{a},

(371d)

\displaystyle\bar{{}\mathbf{c}}^{a}{}\bm{\eta}^{a}\equiv{}

\displaystyle(\bar{c}^{a}+\bar{d}^{a})\eta^{a}+\bar{d}^{a}\kappa^{a}+\bar{e}^{a}\upsilon^{a}+\tilde{\gamma}^{a}J^{a}+\tilde{\varepsilon}^{a}K^{a},

where (
J^{a\,\mu}
,
K^{a\,\mu}
,
L^{a\,\mu}
,
\mathcal{J}^{a\,\mu\nu}
,
\mathcal{K}^{a\,\mu\nu}
,
\mathcal{L}^{a\,\mu\nu}
) are real ordinary sources, (
{J}^{a}
,
{K}^{a}
) are complex ordinary sources, and (
\eta^{a\,\mu}
,
\kappa^{a\,\mu}
,
\Omega^{a\mu\nu}
,
\bar{\Omega}^{a\,\mu\nu}
,
\eta^{a}
,
\kappa^{a}
,
\upsilon^{a}
) are Grassmann sources. The Feynman rules derived from Eq. (
370
) are presented in appendix
10.C
.

The BRST transformation that leaves the action in Eq. (
370
) invariant reads

(372a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}A_{\mu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle D_{\mu}^{ab}(A)c^{b},

(372b)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\lambda_{\mu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle D_{\mu}^{ab}(A)d^{b}+gf^{abc}\lambda^{b}_{\mu}c^{c}+gf^{abc}(\chi^{b}_{\mu}e^{c}+\bar{\theta}^{b}_{\mu}\varepsilon^{c}-\theta^{b}_{\mu}{\gamma}^{c}),

(372c)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\chi_{\mu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle D_{\mu}^{ab}(A)e^{b}+gf^{abc}\chi^{b}c^{c},

(372d)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\bar{\theta}_{\mu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle D_{\mu}^{ab}(A)\gamma^{b}+gf^{abc}\bar{\theta}^{b}c^{c},

(372e)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\theta_{\mu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle D_{\mu}^{ab}(A)\varepsilon^{b}+gf^{abc}{\theta}^{b}c^{c},

(372f)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\mathcal{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle gf^{abc}F_{\mu\nu}^{b}c^{c},

(372g)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\Lambda_{\mu\nu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle gf^{abc}\Lambda_{\mu\nu}^{b}d^{c}+gf^{abc}F_{\mu\nu}^{b}c^{c},

(372h)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\chi_{\mu\nu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle gf^{abc}\chi_{\mu\nu}^{b}c^{c},

(372i)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\bar{\theta}_{\mu\nu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle gf^{abc}\bar{\theta}_{\mu\nu}^{b}c^{c},

(372j)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle gf^{abc}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{b}c^{c},

(372k)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}c_{\mu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{g}{2}f^{abc}c^{b}c^{c}

(372l)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}e_{\mu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle-gf^{abc}c^{b}e^{c}

(372m)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\gamma_{\mu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle-gf^{abc}c^{b}\gamma^{c},

(372n)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\varepsilon_{\mu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle-gf^{abc}c^{b}\varepsilon^{c}

(372o)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}d_{\mu}^{a}={}

\displaystyle-gf^{abc}\left(d^{b}c^{c}+\frac{1}{2}e^{b}e^{c}+\gamma^{b}\varepsilon^{c}\right),

and

(373a)

\begin{split}&\mathsf{s}\bar{c}^{a}={}-\frac{1}{\alpha}\partial\cdot{(A+\lambda)}^{a},\quad\mathsf{s}\bar{d}^{a}={}-\frac{1}{\alpha}\partial\cdot A^{a},\quad\mathsf{s}\bar{e}^{a}={}-\frac{1}{\alpha}\partial\cdot\chi^{a},\\
&\mathsf{s}\tilde{\gamma}^{a}={}-\frac{1}{\alpha}\partial\cdot\theta^{a},\quad\mathsf{s}\tilde{\varepsilon}^{a}={}\frac{1}{\alpha}\partial\cdot\bar{\theta}^{a}.\end{split}

20
Alternative method for a general gauge theory

In this section, we revisit the alternative method (see Section
11.1
) in the framework of the modified LM theory. In this method, the LM fields are introduced after the action is quantized through the FP procedure. We demonstrate that the LM formalism and the FP procedure commute, which means that the LM fields may be introduced either before or after the quantization of the original theory. Consequently, LM fields can be viewed as either pure quantum fields or classical fields.

20.1
Second-order Yang-Mills theory

In this section, we consider the YM theory in the second-order formulation.
Using the FP procedure, we obtain a nonsingular action to the SOYM theory
[
101
]

(374)

\mathfrak{S}_{\text{FP}}=\int\mathop{dx}\left(-\frac{1}{4}(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}^{a}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}^{a}+gf^{abc}A_{\mu}^{b}A_{\nu}^{c})^{2}-\frac{1}{2\alpha}(\partial_{\mu}A^{a\,\mu})^{2}+\bar{c}^{a}\partial^{\mu}D_{\mu}^{ab}c^{b}\right),

where the Lorenz gauge was employed. Following the alternative method presented in Sec.
11.1
, we now introduce LM fields to each field in the action (
374
). This results in the following terms

(375)

\begin{split}gf^{apb}\bar{c}^{a}\partial^{\mu}\lambda^{p}_{\mu}c^{b}+\lambda_{\nu}^{a}D^{ab}_{\mu}(A)F^{b\,\mu\nu}-\frac{\partial^{\mu}\lambda_{\mu}^{a}\partial^{\nu}A^{a}_{\nu}}{\alpha}+\bar{d}^{a}\partial^{\mu}D_{\mu}^{ab}(A)c^{b}+\bar{c}^{a}\partial^{\mu}D_{\mu}^{ab}(A)d^{b},\end{split}

where
F^{a}_{\mu\nu}
is the curvature (
1
) and
\lambda_{\mu}^{a}
,
\bar{d}^{a}
,
d^{a}
are respectively the LM fields corresponding to the fields
A_{\mu}^{a}
,
\bar{c}^{a}
and
c^{a}
.

In the modified LM formalism, the introduction of the LM fields is compensated by inserting the superdeterminant factor
17
17

17

Recall that
\updelta^{2}\equiv\delta_{L}\delta_{R}
.

(376)

\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}\frac{\updelta^{2}\mathfrak{S}_{\text{FP}}}{\delta{\Phi}_{\text{YM}}\delta\Phi_{\text{YM}}}\right)^{+1/2}\equiv\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}\mathbf{H}_{\text{YM}}^{+1/2},

into the path integral, where the superfield
\Phi_{\text{YM}}\equiv(A,c,\bar{c})
contains all of the fields of the nonsingular action (
374
). One can show that the Hessian in Eq. (
376
) is given by

(377)

\mathbf{H}_{\text{YM}}=\begin{pmatrix}B^{uv}_{\mu\nu}&-gf^{aul}({\partial}^{\mu}\bar{c}^{a})&-gf^{muc}c^{c}\overleftarrow{\partial^{\mu}}\\
gf^{avp}({\partial^{\nu}}\bar{c}^{a})&0&-{D}{{}_{\rho}^{mp}(A)}\overleftarrow{\partial^{\rho}}\\
gf^{qvc}\overrightarrow{\partial^{\nu}}c^{c}&\overrightarrow{\partial^{\rho}}{D}_{\rho}^{ql}(A)&0\end{pmatrix}^{1/2},

with

(378)

B_{\mu\nu}^{uv}\equiv\frac{\updelta^{2}\mathfrak{S}_{\text{FP}}}{\delta A^{u\,\mu}\delta A^{v\,\nu}}.

Alternatively, one may use

(379)

\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}\frac{\updelta^{2}\mathfrak{S}_{\text{FP}}}{\delta\bar{\Psi}_{\text{YM}}\delta\Psi_{\text{YM}}}\right)^{+1/2}=\begin{Vmatrix}\frac{1}{2}B^{uv}_{\mu\nu}&-gf^{aul}({\partial}^{\mu}\bar{c}^{a})\\
gf^{qvc}\overrightarrow{\partial^{\nu}}c^{c}&\overrightarrow{\partial^{\rho}}D_{\rho}^{ql}(A)\end{Vmatrix}^{1/2}

with
\Psi_{\text{YM}}=(A,c)
and
\bar{\Psi}_{\text{YM}}=(A,\bar{c})
.

Exponentiating the superdeterminant factor, we find

(380)

\begin{split}\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\mu_{\text{mYM}}}\exp i\int\mathop{dx}\left[\begin{pmatrix}\chi_{\mu}^{u}&\bar{e}^{q}\end{pmatrix}\mathbf{R}_{\text{YM}}\begin{pmatrix}\chi^{v}_{\nu}\\
e^{l}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\theta_{\mu}^{u}&\varepsilon^{p}&\tilde{\gamma}^{q}\end{pmatrix}\mathbf{H}_{\text{YM}}\begin{pmatrix}-\bar{\theta}^{v}_{\nu}\\
\gamma^{l}\\
-\tilde{\varepsilon}^{m}\end{pmatrix}\right]\end{split},

where
\mathop{\mathcal{D}\mu_{\text{mYM}}}\equiv\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi_{\mu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{e}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}e^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\theta}_{\mu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\theta_{\mu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\tilde{\gamma}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\gamma^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\tilde{\varepsilon}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\varepsilon^{a}}
,
\chi_{\mu}^{a}
is a real field;
\bar{e}^{a}
,
e^{a}
,
\bar{\theta}_{\mu}^{a}
,
\theta_{\mu}^{a}
are Grassmann fields;
\bar{\gamma}^{a}
,
\gamma^{a}
,
\bar{\varepsilon}^{a}
,
\varepsilon^{a}
are complex fields. The matrix
\mathbf{H}_{\text{YM}}
is defined in Eq. (
377
) and
\mathbf{R}_{\text{YM}}
is the Hessian in Eq. (
379
).

The total contribution from the Lee-Yang ghost fields is

(381)

\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi_{\mu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{e}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}e^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\theta}_{\mu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\theta_{\mu}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\tilde{\gamma}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\gamma^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\tilde{\varepsilon}^{a}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\varepsilon^{a}}\exp i\int\mathop{dx}\mathcal{L}_{\text{LY}},

where

(382)

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\text{LY}}={}&\frac{1}{2}\chi_{\mu}^{u}B_{\mu\nu}^{uv}\chi_{\nu}^{v}+\bar{\theta}_{\mu}^{u}B_{\mu\nu}^{uv}\theta_{\nu}^{v}+\bar{e}^{u}\partial^{\mu}D_{\mu}^{uv}(A)e^{v}+\tilde{\gamma}^{u}\partial^{\mu}D_{\mu}^{uv}(A)+\tilde{\varepsilon}^{u}\partial^{\mu}D_{\mu}^{uv}(A)\varepsilon^{v}\\
&-gf^{aul}\chi_{\mu}^{u}(\partial^{\mu}\bar{c}^{a})e^{l}+gf^{qvc}\bar{e}^{q}\partial^{\nu}{c}^{c}\chi_{\nu}^{v}-gf^{aul}\theta_{\mu}^{u}(\partial^{\mu}\bar{c}^{a})\gamma^{l}+gf^{muc}\partial^{\nu}(\theta_{\mu}^{u}c^{c})\tilde{\varepsilon}^{m}\\
&-gf^{avp}\varepsilon^{p}(\partial^{\nu}\bar{c}^{a})\theta_{\nu}^{v}-gf^{qvc}\tilde{\gamma}^{q}\partial^{\nu}c^{c}\theta_{\nu}^{v}.\end{split}

Thus, one can show that the total effective Lagrangian can be expressed as

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\left[(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}^{a}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}^{a}+gf^{abc}A_{\mu}^{a}A_{\nu}^{b})^{2}+(\partial_{\mu}\chi_{\nu}^{a}-\partial_{\nu}\chi_{\mu}^{a})^{2}+2(\partial_{\mu}\bar{\theta}_{\nu}^{a}-\partial_{\nu}\bar{\theta}_{\mu}^{a})(\partial^{\mu}\theta^{a\,\nu}-\partial^{\nu}\theta^{a\,\mu})\right]

\displaystyle+\lambda_{\mu}^{a}D^{ab}_{\nu}F^{b\nu\mu}-\frac{1}{2\alpha}\left[(\partial\cdot A^{a})^{2}+2(\partial\cdot\lambda^{a})(\partial\cdot A^{a})+(\partial\cdot\chi^{a})^{2}+2(\partial\cdot\bar{\theta}^{a})(\partial\cdot\theta^{a})\right]

\displaystyle-gf^{abc}(\partial_{\mu}\chi_{\nu}^{a}-\partial_{\nu}\chi_{\mu}^{a})A^{b\,\mu}\chi^{c\,\nu}-\frac{g}{2}f^{abc}(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}^{a}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}^{a})\chi^{b\,\mu}\chi^{c\,\nu}

\displaystyle-\frac{g^{2}}{2}\left[f^{abc}f^{ade}A_{\mu}^{b}\chi_{\nu}^{c}A^{d\,\mu}\chi^{e\,\nu}+f^{abc}f^{ade}A_{\mu}^{b}\chi_{\nu}^{c}\chi^{d\,\mu}A^{e\,\nu}+f^{abc}f^{ade}A_{\mu}^{b}A_{\nu}^{c}\chi^{d\,\mu}\chi^{e\,\nu}\right]

\displaystyle-g\left[f^{abc}(\partial_{\mu}\bar{\theta}_{\nu}^{a}-\partial_{\nu}\bar{\theta}_{\mu}^{a})A^{b\,\mu}\theta^{c\,\nu}+f^{abc}A^{b\,\mu}\bar{\theta}^{c\,\nu}(\partial_{\mu}\theta_{\nu}^{a}-\partial_{\nu}\theta_{\mu}^{a})+f^{abc}(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}^{a}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}^{a})\bar{\theta}^{b\,\mu}\theta^{c\,\nu}\right]

\displaystyle-g^{2}\left[f^{abc}f^{ade}A_{\mu}^{b}\bar{\theta}_{\nu}^{c}A^{d\,\mu}\theta^{e\,\nu}+f^{abc}f^{ade}A_{\mu}^{b}\bar{\theta}_{\nu}^{c}\theta^{d\,\mu}A^{e\,\nu}+f^{abc}f^{ade}A_{\mu}^{b}A_{\nu}^{c}\bar{\theta}^{d\,\mu}\theta^{e\,\nu}\right]

\displaystyle+\bar{c}^{a}\partial\cdot D^{ab}(A+\lambda)c^{b}+\bar{d}^{a}\partial\cdot D^{ab}(A)c^{b}+\bar{c}^{a}\partial\cdot D^{ab}(A)d^{b}+\bar{e}^{a}\partial\cdot D^{ab}(A)e^{b}

\displaystyle+\tilde{\gamma}^{a}\partial\cdot D^{ab}(A)\gamma^{b}+\tilde{\varepsilon}^{a}\partial\cdot D^{ab}(A)\varepsilon^{b}+\bar{c}^{a}gf^{apb}\partial\cdot\chi^{p}e^{b}+\bar{e}^{a}gf^{apb}\partial\cdot\chi^{p}c^{b}

\displaystyle-\bar{c}^{a}gf^{apb}\partial\cdot\theta^{p}\gamma^{b}+\tilde{\gamma}^{a}gf^{apb}\partial\cdot{\bar{\theta}}^{p}c^{b}+\bar{c}^{a}gf^{apb}\partial\cdot{\bar{\theta}}^{p}\varepsilon^{b}+\tilde{\varepsilon}^{a}gf^{apb}\partial\cdot\theta^{p}c^{b},

which is the same obtained in
[
101
]
.

Thus, we explicitly verified that the alternative method introduced in Section
11.1
also holds in the framework of the modified LM formalism.

20.2
General gauge theory

Now, we consider a more general proof for the commutation of the LM formalism and the FP quantization. We use a general gauge theory of YM type.

We will start with the quantization. Let us consider a gauge theory with the classical action given by

(384)

S_{0}=\int\mathop{dx}\mathcal{L}_{0}(\phi).

It is invariant under the gauge transformation

(385)

\delta\phi_{i}=H_{iJ}(\phi)\xi_{J}.

We assume that the gauge algebra is closed and that the condition (
346
) is satisfied by
H_{iJ}(\phi)
and the structure constants
f_{mn|i}
.

To quantize this theory, we have to fix the gauge using some gauge condition
F_{Ij}\phi_{j}=0
.
18
18

18

We assume that the gauge fixing is linear, that is,
F_{Ij,k}=0
.
Employing the FP quantization, we arrive at the FP action:

(386)

S_{\text{FP}}=S_{0}+\int\mathop{dx}\left[\mathcal{L}_{\text{gf}}(\phi)+\mathcal{L}_{\text{gh}}(\phi)\right],

where

(387)

\mathcal{L}_{\text{gf}}(\phi)=-\frac{1}{2\alpha}(F_{Ij}\phi_{j})^{2}

and

(388)

\mathcal{L}_{\text{gh}}(\phi)=\bar{c}_{I}F_{Ij}H_{jK}c_{K}.

Now, we proceed with the LM formalism and supplement the effective action in Eq. (
386
) with the LM field
\lambda_{i}
(commuting field) and
d_{i}
,
\bar{d}_{i}
(anticommuting fields). This yields

(389)

S_{\text{sLM}}=\text{S}_{\text{FP}}+S_{\lambda}+S_{d,\bar{d}},

where

(390)

S_{\lambda}=\lambda_{i}\frac{\delta S_{\text{FP}}}{\delta\phi_{i}}=\lambda_{i}\frac{\delta S_{0}}{\delta\phi_{i}}-\frac{1}{\alpha}F_{Ij}\phi_{j}F_{Ik}\lambda_{k}+\bar{c}_{I}F_{Ij}H_{jK,l}\lambda_{l}c_{K}

and

(391)

S_{d,\bar{d}}=\bar{d}_{I}F_{Ij}H_{jK}c_{K}+\bar{c}_{I}F_{Ij}H_{jK}d_{K}.

The action (
389
) is identical to the action of the standard LM formalism obtained in Ref.
[
30
, Eq. 5.21]
.

In
[
30
]
, the standard method is used. First, the classical action is supplemented with LM fields and then quantized using an extension of the FP procedure. Here, we have interchanged this order. This demonstrates that the FP quantization and the standard LM formalism commute, as we have explicitly verified for gravity in Section
11.1
.

Finally, we introduce the superdeterminant factor

(392)

\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}\mathbf{H}^{+1/2}\equiv\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}\left(\frac{\updelta^{2}S_{\text{FP}}}{\delta{\Phi}\delta\Phi}\right)^{+1/2}\qquad\big[\Phi\equiv(\phi,c,\bar{c})\big]

to obtain the generating functional of the action (
384
) in the framework of the modified LM theory:

(393)

\mathcal{Z}[0]=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\phi_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}c_{I}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{c}_{I}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\lambda_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}d_{I}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{d}_{I}}\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}\mathbf{H}^{+1/2}\exp iS_{\text{sLM}}.

We rewrite the superdeterminant factor as

(394)

\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}\left(\frac{\updelta^{2}S_{\text{FP}}}{\delta{\Phi}\delta\Phi}\right)^{+1/2}=\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{Sdet}}\mathbf{H}^{+1},

where

(395)

\mathbf{H}=\begin{pmatrix}B_{ij}&\bar{c}_{I}\overleftrightarrow{O}_{IL|i}&-c_{K}\overleftarrow{O}\!_{MK|i}\\
-\bar{c}_{I}\overleftrightarrow{O}_{IP|j}&0&-F_{Ma}H_{aP}\\
\overrightarrow{O}\!_{QK|j}c_{K}&F_{Qa}H_{aL}&0\end{pmatrix}^{1/2},\qquad\left(B_{ij}\equiv\frac{\updelta^{2}S_{\text{FP}}}{\delta\phi_{i}\delta\phi_{j}}\right)

and
O_{IL|i}\equiv F_{Ik}H_{kL,i}
. The arrows indicate in which fields the operator
O
acts. The operator
\overleftrightarrow{O}
does not act on
\bar{c}_{I}
.

The superdeterminant factors in Eq. (
394
) can be exponentiated using ghost fields yielding

(396)

\begin{split}\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\mu_{\text{LY}}}\exp i\int\mathop{dx}\left[\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\chi_{i}&{e}_{P}&\bar{e}_{Q}\end{pmatrix}\mathbf{H}\begin{pmatrix}\chi_{j}\\
e_{L}\\
\bar{e}_{M}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}\theta_{i}&\varepsilon_{P}&\tilde{\gamma}_{Q}\end{pmatrix}\mathbf{H}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\theta}_{j}\\
-\gamma_{L}\\
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{M}\end{pmatrix}\right]\end{split},

where
\mathop{\mathcal{D}\mu_{\text{LY}}}\equiv\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{e}_{I}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}e_{I}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\theta}_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\theta_{i}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\tilde{\gamma}_{I}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\gamma_{I}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{I}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\varepsilon_{I}}
,
\chi_{i}
is a real field;
\bar{e}_{I}
,
e_{I}
,
\bar{\theta}_{i}
,
\theta_{i}
are Grassmann fields;
\bar{\gamma}_{I}
,
\gamma_{I}
,
\bar{\varepsilon}_{I}
,
\varepsilon_{I}
are complex fields. The Lee-Yang ghost Lagrangian (
396
) can be rewritten as

(397)

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\text{LY}}(\phi)={}&\frac{1}{2}\chi_{i}B_{ij}\chi_{j}+\bar{\theta}_{i}B_{ij}\theta_{j}+\bar{e}_{I}F_{Il}H_{lJ}e_{J}+\tilde{\gamma}_{I}F_{Il}H_{lJ}\gamma_{J}+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{I}F_{Il}H_{lJ}\varepsilon_{J}\\
&+\bar{c}_{I}F_{Ik}H_{kL,i}\chi_{i}e_{L}+\bar{e}_{M}F_{Ml}H_{lK,i}\chi_{i}c_{K}-\bar{c}_{I}F_{Ik}H_{kL,i}\theta_{i}\gamma_{L}\\
&+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{M}F_{Ml}H_{lK,i}\theta_{i}c_{K}+\bar{c}_{I}F_{Ik}H_{kP,j}\bar{\theta}_{j}\varepsilon_{P}+\tilde{\gamma}_{Q}F_{Ql}H_{lK,j}\bar{\theta}_{j}c_{K}.\end{split}

Adding the Lee-Yang ghost Lagrangian (
374
) to the standard LM Lagrangian (see Eq. (
389
)) results in the effective Lagrangian of the modified LM formalism:

(398)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{L}_{\text{T}}=\mathcal{L}_{0}(\phi)+\mathcal{L}_{\text{gf}}(\phi)+\mathcal{L}_{\text{gh}}(\phi)+\lambda_{i}\frac{\delta S_{0}}{\delta\phi_{i}}-\frac{1}{\alpha}F_{Ij}\phi_{j}F_{Ik}\lambda_{k}\\
&+\bar{c}_{I}F_{Ij}H_{jK,l}\lambda_{l}c_{K}+\bar{d}_{I}F_{Ij}H_{jK}c_{K}+\bar{c}_{I}F_{Ij}H_{jK}d_{K}+\mathcal{L}_{\text{LY}}(\phi).\end{split}

This Lagrangian (
398
) is equal to the total effective action obtained with the standard method in Eq. (
360
), which confirms that the LM formalism commutes with the FP quantization formalism.

21
Additional symmetries

Here, we extend the symmetries shown in Section
15
. The Lagrangian of the modified LM theory in Eq. (
398
) is invariant under

(399a)

\displaystyle\delta\bar{\theta}_{i}=-\sigma_{\text{LM}}\bar{\theta}_{i},

\displaystyle\delta\theta_{i}=\sigma_{\text{LM}}\theta_{i}

(399b)

\displaystyle\delta\bar{c}_{I}=-\sigma_{\text{FP}}\bar{c}_{I},

\displaystyle\delta c_{I}=-\sigma_{\text{FP}}c_{I},

(399c)

\displaystyle\delta\bar{d}_{I}=-\sigma_{\text{FP}}\bar{d}_{I},

\displaystyle\delta d_{I}=-\sigma_{\text{FP}}d_{I},

(399d)

\displaystyle\delta\bar{e}_{I}=-\sigma_{\text{FP}}\bar{e}_{I},

\displaystyle\delta e_{I}=\sigma_{\text{FP}}e_{I},

(399e)

\displaystyle\delta\tilde{\gamma}_{I}=-(\sigma_{\text{FP}}-\sigma_{\text{LM}})\tilde{\gamma}_{I},

\displaystyle\delta\gamma_{I}=(\sigma_{\text{FP}}-\sigma_{\text{LM}})\gamma_{I},

(399f)

\displaystyle\delta\tilde{\varepsilon}_{I}=-(\sigma_{\text{FP}}+\sigma_{\text{LM}})\tilde{\varepsilon}_{I},

\displaystyle\delta\varepsilon_{I}=(\sigma_{\text{FP}}+\sigma_{\text{LM}})\varepsilon_{I},

(399g)

\displaystyle\delta\phi_{i}=\delta\lambda_{i}=\delta\chi_{i}=0;

where
\sigma_{\text{LM}}
and
\sigma_{\text{FP}}
are commuting parameters. We find that the ghost number symmetries of both the modified LM and the FP ghost fields are compatible. This extended symmetry allows us to classify each field by its charge. We observe that the commuting complex fields
(\tilde{\gamma}_{I},\gamma_{i})
and
(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{I},\epsilon_{I})
carry both the LM and FP ghost charges, respectively,
\sigma_{\text{LM}}
and
\sigma_{\text{FP}}
.

We also have the extended (anti)BRST-like symmetry:

(400)

\displaystyle\delta\theta_{i}=-\rho_{+}\chi_{i}\eta,

\displaystyle\delta\bar{\theta}_{i}=\rho_{-}\chi_{i}\eta,

\displaystyle\delta\chi_{i}=(\rho_{-}\theta_{i}+\bar{\theta}_{i}\rho_{+})\eta,

(401)

\displaystyle\delta{{\varepsilon}}_{I}=\rho_{+}{e}_{I}\eta,

\displaystyle\delta\gamma_{I}=\rho_{-}e_{I}\eta,

\displaystyle\delta e_{I}=-({{\varepsilon}}_{I}\rho_{-}+\rho_{+}{\gamma}_{I})\eta,

(402)

\displaystyle\delta\tilde{\gamma}_{I}=\rho_{+}\bar{e}_{I}\eta,

\displaystyle\delta\tilde{{\varepsilon}}_{I}=\rho_{-}\bar{e}_{I}\eta,

\displaystyle\delta\bar{e}_{I}=(\tilde{{\varepsilon}}_{I}\rho_{+}+\rho_{-}\tilde{\gamma}_{I})\eta,

(403)

\displaystyle\delta\phi_{i}=\delta\lambda_{i}=0,

\displaystyle\delta c_{I}=\delta d_{I}=0,

\displaystyle\delta\bar{c}_{I}=\delta\bar{d}_{I}=0;

where
\eta
is an anticommuting parameter, and
\rho_{\pm}
is a real parameter. When
\rho_{\pm}=0
, we find the pure (anti)BRST-like symmetry. This is a internal symmetry of the Lee-Yang-like ghost sector itself, leaving the standard LM formalism fields unaffected.

Chapter 8
Discussion

In this work, we studied the first-order formulation of the YM theory and gravity. In particular, the quantum equivalence between these formulations using the path integral quantization procedure. We have shown that we can obtain identities that relate Green’s functions of the auxiliary field to Green’s functions in the second-order formulation of composite fields. These structural identities, which are directly associated with the quantum equivalence between those formalisms, are complementary to the standard Ward identities.

On the other hand, the first-order formalism has additional symmetries due to the auxiliary fields which lead to similar identities. However, we also have shown that one cannot obtain the equivalence between Green’s functions of the gauge field from the Ward identities derived from these symmetries.

We have also investigated the quantum equivalence using other generating functionals, extending the previous works
[
9
,
10
]
. We have demonstrated that we can show the quantum equivalence via the effective action
\Gamma
, which provides a proper definition of self-energy in the first-order formulation. In YM theory, we have shown that the self-energy of the field
A_{\mu}^{a}
remains identical in both formulations. The proper self-energy in the first-order formalism is obtained from the naive self-energies defined as the sum of 1PI diagrams with two amputated external legs. This resolves the apparent inconsistency of the self-energies computed in the first-order formalism.

At finite temperature, the proper self-energy of the field
A_{\mu}^{a}
, at the high-temperature limit, is identical in both formulations of the YM theory
[
72
]
. If Eq. (
232
) holds, then

(404)

-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{A\mathcal{F}}\mathbf{D}^{-1}\mathbf{Q}_{-}-\mathbf{Q}_{+}\mathbf{D}^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{F}A}+\mathbf{Q}_{+}\mathbf{D}^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{F}}\mathbf{D}^{-1}\mathbf{Q}_{-}=0

at the high-temperature limit. Recall that
\mathbf{\Sigma}
represents the sum of 1PI diagrams with two amputated legs and
\mathbf{Q}_{\pm}
and
\mathbf{D}
are components of the Hessian of the FOYM action (see Eq. (
219
)).
19
19

19

The identity (
404
) can be written in component-form:
\Sigma_{\mu}^{ac\,\alpha\beta}Q_{\alpha\beta\,\nu}^{cb}+Q_{\mu}^{ac\,\alpha\beta}\Sigma_{\alpha\beta\,\nu}^{cb}+Q^{ac}_{\mu\,\alpha\beta}\Sigma^{cd\,\alpha\beta\rho\sigma}Q^{db}_{\rho\sigma\,\nu}=0
(for definitions, see Section
8.4
).
Indeed, the identity (
404
) is valid, which can be directly verified by using the results in Ref.
[
72
]
.

In Ref.
[
72
]
, it is also shown that the self-energies involving the auxiliary field
\mathcal{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a}
and the FP ghost fields
c^{a}
,
\bar{c}^{a}
are sub-leading in the high-temperature limit. Despite the quantum equivalence, the decomposition of Green’s functions as in Eq. (
232
) is not arbitrary. The auxiliary field has a physical interpretation richer than initially expected. This is an important subject that may be investigated in the future.

We have provided a systematic procedure to obtain the path integral of the first-order formalism through the path integral of the second-order formalism. This is particularly interesting in gravity since it gives the Senjanović determinant in a manifestly covariant form. With the generating functional of the FOGR theory obtained from this procedure, we have shown that the structural identities can be verified at the integrand level. This can be understood as follow: in the naive path integral, there are tadpole-like contributions arising from the determinant factor
(\det M(h))^{-1/2}
, which are exactly canceled by contributions coming from the Senjanović determinant proposed in Eq. (
154
).

Moreover, this path integral is better suitable at finite temperature, since the contributions from the Senjanović determinant may become relevant (see Appendix
12
). Nevertheless, it is essential to account for second-class constraints in the first-order formalism to count correctly the degrees of freedom of the theory.

We also have demonstrated that first-order formulations of the YM theory with non-minimal couplings can be systematically derived. These couplings depend on the auxiliary fields, specifically the curvature
F_{\mu\nu}^{a}
. These first-order formulations are equivalent to their corresponding second-order formulations, including at the quantum level. This procedure can be easily generalized to gravity. In this case, it is most relevant since the minimal coupling of fermions to gravity leads to terms that are dependent on the auxiliary field, the connection
\Gamma^{\lambda}{}_{\mu\nu}
. This would result in a first-order formulation of gravity coupled to fermions that is quantum equivalent to the SOGR theory in any dimension
D>2
.
20
20

20

This restriction is because of the nature of the HE action. In
D=2
, the HE action is purely topological.

In YM theory, we have the Pauli interaction (in the fundamental representation)

(405)

\bar{\psi}T^{c}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{c}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{c}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{c}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{c}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma_{\alpha\beta}\psi.

In gravity, the fermion is minimally coupled by the covariant derivative that leads to a similar term

(406)

\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}\mathchoice{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ab}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ab}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ab}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ab}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma_{ab}\psi,

where
\mathchoice{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ab}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ab}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ab}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\omega^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ab}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
is the spin connection and
\gamma^{\mu}=\gamma^{a}\mathchoice{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
. The following relation holds between them:

(407)

ab\longleftrightarrow\alpha\beta,\quad\mu\longleftrightarrow c,\quad\omega\longleftrightarrow F\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma\longleftrightarrow T.

Thus, our investigation of the YM theory can be directly translated to gravity using this analogy. In contrast, the Palatini approach leads to an inequivalent theory. This theory is the well-known Einstein-Cartan theory, which describes more general spacetimes with torsion and curvature.

We also have presented an alternative gravity theory that is renormalizable and unitary. This theory is obtained from the standard LM formalism in which LM fields are introduced to constrain the path integral restricting the loop expansion to one-loop order. We have shown that this model is consistent with the quantum equivalence between the first- and second-order formulations of gravity. This model coupled to matter fields was investigated in
[
31
]
.
This could provide another way to address the issue of coupling fermions to the FOGR theory, without breaking its equivalence to the SOGR theory. We hope to investigate this in the future.

In
[
37
]
, we have identified the lack of field redefinition invariance in the path integral of the standard LM formalism. To address this issue, we propose introducing a determinant factor into its measure to restore this invariance. This modification effectively resolves several drawbacks of the standard LM formalism, particularly the doubling of degrees of freedom and the additional one-loop contributions from the LM fields.

The determinant factor can be exponentiated using ghost fields, which are analogous to the Lee-Yang ghost fields in the worldline formalism
[
99
,
100
]
.
These ghosts are responsible for canceling the additional one-loop contributions arising from the LM fields. We have provided a diagrammatic analysis to verify our results. In particular, we demonstrated that the ghost fields do not spoil the restriction of the loop expansion to one-loop order. In addition, they are responsible for removing the unphysical contributions coming from the LM fields. We have shown that the LM fields are linked to Ostrogradsky instabilities, which can lead to an unbounded Hamiltonian and break the unitarity of the theory.

In the framework of the modified LM formalism, this issue is resolved, since the Lee-Yang-like ghosts cancel out unphysical states associated with the Ostrogradsky ghosts. Indeed, we have shown that the degrees of freedom associated with the Ostrogradsky ghosts are removed effectively by the Lee-Yang-like ghost fields. We also recall that this issue could be resolved using the indefinite metric quantization approach, as shown in Ref.
[
31
]
.

Finally, we have extended the modified LM formalism to theories with singular actions. We have shown that the Lee-Yang-like ghosts become gauge fields and the FP quantization must be extended to accommodate these additional gauge fields. This implies that we need to introduce ghosts of ghosts in order to obtain a consistent covariant path integral. We have presented the corresponding BRST symmetry of the resulting effective action. This procedure is applied to the first-order formulation of the YM theory, and the Feynman rules are detailed in Appendix
10.C
. At the end, we have established that the LM formalism commutes with the FP quantization. This implies that the LM fields can be viewed as purely quantum fields ensuring that the classical equations of motion are preserved.

One can use the Hilbert-Einstein action within this framework to derive a quantum gravity theory that is both renormalizable and unitary. The classical limit of this theory should yield GR, and the quantum effects are restricted to one-loop order. This theory has two degrees of freedom corresponding to the two polarizations of the graviton, which are identical to the degrees of freedom of the Hilbert-Einstein gravity theory.

Chapter 9
Derivation of Eq. (
27
)

In this appendix, we use a different notation than the rest of the work:
{F}\to G
. Thus, Eq. (
27
) can be rewritten as

(408)

G^{a\,\mu\nu}{\star}{G}^{a}_{\mu\nu}=\partial_{\mu}K^{\mu},

where

(409)

K^{\mu}=\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\left(A_{\nu}^{a}{G}^{a}_{\rho\sigma}-\frac{2}{3}gf^{abc}A_{\nu}^{a}A_{\rho}^{b}{A}_{\sigma}^{c}\right).

Recall that
{\star}G^{a\mu\nu}=\epsilon^{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}G^{a}_{\alpha\beta}/2
.
In the Abelian case (
g=0
), the first term in the left-hand side of Eq. (
27
) yields

(410)

\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}^{a}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}^{a})G^{a}_{\rho\sigma}=G^{a}_{\mu\nu}{\star}{G}^{a\,\mu\nu},

while the second term vanishes

(411)

-A_{\nu}\epsilon^{\nu\mu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\mu}G_{\rho\sigma}=0,

since
G_{\rho\sigma}
satisfies the Bianchi identity

(412)

\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\nu}G_{\rho\sigma}=\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}(\partial_{\nu}G_{\rho\sigma}+\partial_{\rho}G_{\sigma\nu}+\partial_{\sigma}G_{\nu\rho})=0.

To demonstrate it we only needed the definition of
G
(see Eq. (
1
)) and the Bianchi identity (
412
). It is also true (with appropriate modifications) in the general non-Abelian case.

In the general case (non-Abelian), the RHS of Eq. (
27
) yields

(413)

F^{a}_{\mu\nu}{\star}{G}^{a\,\mu\nu}+A_{\nu}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\mu}G_{\rho\sigma}-\frac{g}{3}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\nu}(f^{abc}A_{\nu}^{a}A_{\rho}^{b}A_{\sigma}^{c}).

We see that the contribution in Eq. (
410
) is now equal to
F^{a}_{\mu\nu}{\star}{G}^{a\,\mu\nu}
, where
F^{a}_{\mu\nu}=G^{a}_{\mu\nu}-gf^{abc}A^{b}_{\mu}A^{c}_{\nu}
(Abelian part of the field strength). Besides that, the second term in Eq. (
413
) does not vanish since the Bianchi identity now is given by

(414)

A_{\nu}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}D_{\mu}G_{\rho\sigma}=0.

By Eq. (
413
), we have that Eq. (
408
) is valid as long as

(415)

-gf^{abc}A_{\mu}^{b}A_{\nu}^{c}{\star}{G}_{\rho\sigma}^{a}+A_{\nu}^{a}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\nu}G_{\rho\sigma}^{a}-\frac{1}{3}g\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\nu}\left(f^{abc}A_{\nu}^{a}A_{\rho}^{b}A_{\sigma}^{c}\right)=0.

Using the Bianchi identity (
414
), ignoring the terms of order
g^{2}
that vanish

(416)

g^{2}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}A^{b}_{\mu}A^{c}_{\nu}A^{l}_{\rho}A^{m}_{\sigma}\left(f^{abc}f^{alm}-f^{abl}f^{acm}+f^{abm}f^{acl}\right)=0

by using the Jacobi identity, in Eq. (
415
); we end up with

(417)

gf^{abc}A_{\mu}^{b}A_{\nu}^{c}{\star}{F}_{\rho\sigma}-\frac{1}{3}g\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\mu}\left(f^{abc}A_{\nu}^{a}A_{\rho}^{b}A_{\sigma}^{c}\right),

which vanishes since

(418)

\begin{split}g\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\mu}\left(f^{abc}A_{\nu}^{a}A_{\rho}^{b}A_{\sigma}^{c}\right)&=g\epsilon^{\rho\sigma\mu\nu}\partial_{\rho}\left(f^{abc}A_{\sigma}^{a}A^{b}_{\mu}A^{c}_{\nu}\right)\\
&=\frac{g}{2}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}f^{abc}\partial_{[\rho}A_{\sigma]}^{a}A^{b}_{\mu}A^{c}_{\nu}+\text{ perms.}\\
&=3{\star}{F}_{\rho\sigma}^{a}gf^{abc}A_{\mu}^{b}A_{\nu}^{c}.\end{split}

Chapter 10
Feynman rules

In this appendix, we present the Feynman rules used in this work. For more details, we refer to
[
64
,
9
,
10
,
11
]
.
In our convention, all vertex momenta are directed inward, and their sum vanishes.

Appendix 10.A
Yang-Mills theory

The propagators in the SOYM (see Section
4
) are given by

(419a)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{i}{p^{2}}\left(\eta_{\mu\nu}-(1-\alpha)\frac{p_{\mu}p_{\nu}}{p^{2}}\right)\delta^{ab},

(419b)

\displaystyle\qquad\frac{i}{p^{2}}\delta^{ab}.

The interaction vertices are

(420a)

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={0.65}]{fig/FR/AAAvertex-eps-converted-to.pdf}}}\qquad V^{abc}_{\mu\nu\rho}(k,p,q),

(420b)

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={0.6}]{fig/FR/vertexAAAA-eps-converted-to.pdf}}}\qquad V^{abcd}_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma},

(420c)

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={0.6}]{fig/FR/ghostvertex-eps-converted-to.pdf}}}\qquad gf^{abc}p_{\mu},

where

(421)

V^{abc}_{\mu\nu\rho}(k,p,q)=gf^{abc}\left[\eta_{\mu\nu}(k-p)_{\rho}+\eta_{\nu\rho}(p-q)_{\mu}+\eta_{\rho\mu}(q-k)_{\nu}\right]

and

(422)

\begin{split}V^{abcd}_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\equiv-i&g^{2}\big[f^{abe}f^{cde}(\eta_{\mu\rho}\eta_{\nu\sigma}-\eta_{\mu\sigma}\eta_{\nu\rho})\\
&\quad+f^{ace}f^{bde}(\eta_{\mu\nu}\eta_{\rho\sigma}-\eta_{\mu\sigma}\eta_{\rho\nu})\\
&\quad+f^{ade}f^{bce}(\eta^{\mu\nu}\eta^{\rho\sigma}-\eta_{\mu\rho}\eta_{\nu\sigma})\big].\end{split}

The spring lines represent the gauge field
A
, and the dotted lines the ghosts
c
and
\bar{c}
.

In the FOYM (see Section
5
), we have the following Feynman rules:

(423a)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i}{p^{2}}\left(\eta_{\mu\nu}-(1-\alpha)\frac{p_{\mu}p_{\nu}}{p^{2}}\right)\delta^{ab},

(423b)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{p_{\lambda}\eta_{\sigma\mu}-p_{\sigma}\eta_{\lambda\mu}}{p^{2}}\delta^{ab},

(423c)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{p_{\alpha}\eta_{\beta\nu}-p_{\beta}\eta_{\alpha\nu}}{p^{2}}\delta^{ab},

(423d)

\displaystyle\quad\quad 2i[I_{\alpha\beta\lambda\sigma}+L_{\alpha\beta\lambda\sigma}(p)]\delta^{ab},

(423e)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\frac{i}{p^{2}}\delta^{ab},

where

(424)

L_{\alpha\beta\lambda\sigma}(p)=-\frac{1}{2p^{2}}\left(p^{\alpha}p^{\lambda}\eta^{\beta\sigma}-p^{\beta}p^{\lambda}\eta^{\alpha\sigma}-p^{\alpha}p^{\sigma}\eta^{\beta\lambda}+p^{\beta}p^{\sigma}\eta^{\alpha\lambda}\right),

(425)

I^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\equiv\frac{\eta^{\mu\rho}\eta^{\nu\sigma}-\eta^{\mu\sigma}\eta^{\nu\rho}}{2}.

The auxiliary field
F^{a\,\mu\nu}
is represented by solid lines.

The vertices reads

(426a)

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={0.65}]{fig/FR/vertexFAA-eps-converted-to.pdf}}}\qquad\mathcal{V}^{abc}_{\mu\nu\,\rho\sigma},

(426b)

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={0.6}]{fig/FR/ghostvertex-eps-converted-to.pdf}}}\quad\quad gf^{abc}p_{\mu};

where

(427)

\mathcal{V}^{abc}_{\mu\nu\,\rho\sigma}=-igf^{abc}I_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}.

Recall that these rules are only valid in the Lorenz gauge
\partial_{\mu}A^{a\mu}=0
.

Appendix 10.B
Gravity

The propagator of the ghost in the De Donder gauge is given by

(428)

\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={0.6}]{fig/FR/propghostPH-eps-converted-to.pdf}}}\qquad-\frac{i}{p^{2}}\eta^{\mu\nu}

and the ghost vertex by

(429)

\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={0.6}]{fig/FR/ghostvertexEH-eps-converted-to.pdf}}}\qquad\frac{i\kappa}{2}\left[\eta_{\pi\tau}(q^{\mu}k^{\nu}+q^{\nu}k^{\mu})-q_{\tau}(p^{\mu}\delta_{\pi}^{\nu}+p^{\nu}\delta_{\pi}^{\mu})\right].

The graviton field
\phi^{\mu\nu}
is represented by wavy lines. The auxiliary field
H_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}
of the diagonal first-order formulation of gravity is represented by solid lines. The ghost fields
d_{\mu}
,
\bar{d}_{\mu}
are represented by dotted lines.

The remaining rules of the diagonal FOGR (see Section
7.3
) are

(430a)

\displaystyle\qquad\mathcal{P}^{\mu\nu\pi\tau}(p),

(430b)

\displaystyle\qquad\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}},

(430c)

\displaystyle\qquad\mathchoice{V^{{{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}}{V^{{{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}}{V^{{{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}}{V^{{{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\pi\tau}{\mu\nu}}}},

(430d)

\displaystyle\qquad\mathchoice{V^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\pi\tau\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{V^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\pi\tau\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{V^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\pi\tau\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{V^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\pi\tau\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}(q,k),

(430e)

\displaystyle\qquad V^{\mu\nu\pi\tau\alpha\beta}(p,q,k);

where the propagators are

(431)

\begin{split}\mathcal{P}^{\mu\nu\pi\tau}(k)={}&-i\frac{\eta^{\pi\nu}\eta^{\mu\tau}+\eta^{\pi\mu}\eta^{\nu\tau}-(2-\alpha)\eta^{\pi\tau}\eta^{\mu\nu}}{k^{2}}\\
&+i(1-\alpha)\frac{k^{\pi}k^{\mu}\eta^{\nu\tau}+k^{\pi}k^{\nu}\eta^{\mu\tau}+\eta^{\pi\nu}k^{\mu}k^{\tau}+\eta^{\pi\mu}k^{\nu}k^{\tau}}{k^{2}},\\
&-2i(1-\alpha)\frac{\eta^{\pi\tau}k^{\mu}k^{\nu}+k^{\pi}k^{\tau}\eta^{\mu\nu}}{k^{2}},\end{split}

(432)

\begin{split}\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}_{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}}={}&\frac{i\kappa^{2}}{4}\eta^{\lambda\rho}\left(\eta_{\pi\mu}\eta_{\tau\nu}+\eta_{\pi\nu}\eta_{\tau\mu}-\frac{2}{(D-2)}\eta_{\mu\nu}\eta_{\pi\tau}\right)\\
&-\frac{i\kappa^{2}}{4}\left(\eta_{\tau\mu}\delta_{\nu}^{\rho}\delta_{\pi}^{\lambda}+\eta_{\pi\mu}\delta_{\nu}^{\rho}\delta_{\tau}^{\lambda}+\eta_{\tau\nu}\delta_{\mu}^{\rho}\delta_{\pi}^{\lambda}+\eta_{\pi\nu}\delta_{\mu}^{\rho}\delta_{\tau}^{\lambda}\right);\end{split}

and the vertices by

(433)

\begin{split}V_{\pi\tau}^{\rho}{}{}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}{}^{\alpha\beta}={}&\frac{i}{8\kappa}\left\{\left[\left(\frac{\delta^{\rho}_{\pi}\delta^{\alpha}_{\nu}\delta^{\beta}_{\tau}\delta^{\lambda}_{\mu}}{D-1}-\delta^{\lambda}_{\pi}\delta^{\alpha}_{\nu}\delta^{\beta}_{\tau}\delta^{\rho}_{\mu}+\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\right)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\right]+\pi\leftrightarrow\tau\right\}\\
&+(\mu\nu,\lambda)\leftrightarrow(\pi\tau,\rho),\end{split}

(434)

\begin{split}V^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}{}^{\pi\tau\alpha\beta}(q,k)={}&\frac{k^{\theta}}{4}\left\{\left[\left(\frac{\delta^{\pi}_{\mu}\delta_{\nu}^{\lambda}}{D-1}\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\tau\rho}}}_{{{\theta}\kern 5.92416pt{\rho}\kern 3.68787pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\tau\rho}}}_{{{\theta}\kern 5.92416pt{\rho}\kern 3.68787pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\tau\rho}}}_{{{\theta}\kern 5.13432pt{\rho}\kern 3.40804pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\tau\rho}}}_{{{\theta}\kern 5.13432pt{\rho}\kern 3.40804pt}}}-\delta^{\pi}_{\mu}\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\tau\lambda}}}_{{{\theta}\kern 5.92416pt{\nu}\kern 4.42166pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\tau\lambda}}}_{{{\theta}\kern 5.92416pt{\nu}\kern 4.42166pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\tau\lambda}}}_{{{\theta}\kern 5.13432pt{\nu}\kern 4.04924pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\tau\lambda}}}_{{{\theta}\kern 5.13432pt{\nu}\kern 4.04924pt}}}+\pi\leftrightarrow\tau\right)+\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\right]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\right\}\\
&+(k,\alpha\beta)\leftrightarrow(q,\pi\tau),\end{split}

(435)

\begin{split}V^{\mu\nu\pi\tau\alpha\beta}(p,q,k)={}&-i\kappa\frac{q^{\kappa}k^{\theta}}{8}\Bigg\{\left[\left(\frac{\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\mu\rho}}}_{{{\theta}\kern 5.92416pt{\rho}\kern 4.86232pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\mu\rho}}}_{{{\theta}\kern 5.92416pt{\rho}\kern 4.86232pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\mu\rho}}}_{{{\theta}\kern 5.13432pt{\rho}\kern 4.24889pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\mu\rho}}}_{{{\theta}\kern 5.13432pt{\rho}\kern 4.24889pt}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{\pi\tau}{\nu\gamma}}}_{{{\kappa}\kern 3.68243pt{\gamma}\kern 4.00928pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\pi\tau}{\nu\gamma}}}_{{{\kappa}\kern 3.68243pt{\gamma}\kern 4.00928pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\pi\tau}{\nu\gamma}}}_{{{\kappa}\kern 3.42598pt{\gamma}\kern 3.56023pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\pi\tau}{\nu\gamma}}}_{{{\kappa}\kern 3.42598pt{\gamma}\kern 3.56023pt}}}}{D-1}-\mathchoice{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\mu\rho}}}_{{{\kappa}\kern 5.05142pt{\gamma}\kern 4.81079pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\mu\rho}}}_{{{\kappa}\kern 5.05142pt{\gamma}\kern 4.81079pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\mu\rho}}}_{{{\kappa}\kern 4.38197pt{\gamma}\kern 4.1446pt}}}{\mathcal{D}^{{{\alpha\beta}{\mu\rho}}}_{{{\kappa}\kern 4.38197pt{\gamma}\kern 4.1446pt}}}\mathcal{D}^{\pi\tau}_{\theta}{}^{\nu\gamma}_{\rho}+\pi\leftrightarrow\tau\right)+\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\right]\\
&+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\Bigg\}+(p,\mu\nu)\leftrightarrow(q,\pi\tau)+(p,\mu\nu)\leftrightarrow(k,\alpha\beta).\end{split}

The symbol
\leftrightarrow
denotes the first term with an interchange of indices (and momenta).

The FOGR (see Section
7
) Feynman rules are given in Ref.

, Eqs. (28–30)]
.

Chapter 11
First-Order formulation of the scalar theory

In this short appendix, we present the first-order formulation of the standard Lagrangian for a spin-0 field
\phi
with potential
V(\phi)
.

The second-order Lagrangian is given by

(436)

\mathcal{L}_{\text{scalar}}^{(2)}=\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{\mu}\phi)^{2}-V(\phi).

Then, the first-order Lagrangian will be expressed as

(437)

\mathcal{L}_{\text{scalar}}^{(1)}=-\frac{1}{2}G_{\mu}G^{\mu}+G_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\phi-V(\phi).

The field
G_{\mu}
is an auxiliary field analog to
\mathcal{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a}
of the FOYM theory.

The quantum equivalence can be verified straightforwardly. We need to shift the field
G_{\mu}
in the generating functional of the first-order spin-0 theory by its classical value

(438)

G_{\mu}\to G_{\mu}+\partial_{\mu}\phi+J_{\mu},

where
J_{\mu}
is the source of the auxiliary field. This shift is analog to Eq. (
173
). The resulting generating functional will be analog to Eq. (
174
), which demonstrates the quantum equivalence between the two formulations. Besides that, structural identities can also be obtained, for instance,

(439)

\langle 0|T\phi G_{\mu}|0\rangle=\langle 0|T\phi\partial_{\mu}\phi|0\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad\langle 0|TG_{\mu}G_{\nu}|0\rangle=\langle 0|T\partial_{\mu}\phi\partial_{\nu}\phi|0\rangle-i\eta_{\mu\nu}.

Chapter 12
Dimensional regularization of massless tadpoles

In this appendix, we compute a massless tadpole-like diagram (a loop diagram independent of external momenta) at finite temperature, and at zero temperature
T=0
, using the imaginary time formalism
[
69
]
. To achieve this, we apply dimensional regularization to regularize loop integrals in the
\phi^{4}
theory. This comparison illustrates that massless tadpole-like diagrams, which vanish at zero temperature when using dimensional regularization, may not vanish at finite temperature.

We consider the one-loop tadpole-like diagram in Fig.
19
. This is the one-loop contribution to the self-energy of the scalar field
\phi
in the standard massless
\phi^{4}
theory described by the action

(440)

S_{\phi^{4}}=\int\mathop{d^{4}x}\left(\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mu}\phi\partial^{\mu}\phi-\frac{\lambda}{4!}\phi^{4}\right).

The Feynman rules required to compute the diagram in Fig.
19
are presented in Fig.
20
.

Figure 19:
One-loop tadpole-like diagram in the
\phi^{4}
theory. Solid lines represent the scalar field
\phi
.

Figure 20:
Feynman rules in the massless
\phi^{4}
theory at finite and zero temperature. The Matsubara frequencies are defined as
\omega_{n}=2\pi nT
(
k_{B}=1)
, where
n
is an integer
[
69
]
. Note that,
p^{2}\equiv p_{\mu}p^{\mu}=p_{0}^{2}-\mathbf{p}^{2}
,
\mathbf{p}
is the momentum vector. However, in the imaginary time formalism (after a Wick rotation
p_{0}\to ip_{0}
) we have that
-p_{E}^{2}=p_{0}^{2}+\mathbf{p}^{2}
.

Appendix 12.A
Zero temperature

At zero temperature, we can use the Feynman rules given in Fig.
20
to compute the diagram in Fig.
19
as

(441)

I[D]=\frac{\lambda}{2}\int\frac{\mathop{d^{D}p}}{(2\pi)^{D}}\frac{1}{p^{2}-M^{2}},

where
1/2
is the combinatorial factor and
M
is a mass parameter. Note that, we extended the loop integral to
D
dimensional which is required to proceed with the dimensional regularization. To obtain the correct result, we set
M=0
at the end of the computation of Eq. (
441
), since the
\phi^{4}
theory described by EQ. (
440
) is massless.

In order to compute the integral
I[D]
, we can use a Wick rotation:
p^{2}=-p_{E}^{2}
and
d^{d}p=id^{D}p_{E}
. After some algebra, we arrive at
[
11
, Eq. (D.10)]

(442)

I[D]=-i\frac{2\pi^{D/2}}{\Gamma(D/2)}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{D}}M^{D-2}\frac{\Gamma(1-D/2)}{\Gamma(1)}\to 0,

where the arrow indicates its value when
M=0
(we set
D=4-2\epsilon
in which
\epsilon
is a small nonzero value). This shows that the tadpole-like diagram in Fig.
19
vanishes using dimensional regularization at zero temperature.

Now, note that the scaleless integral

(443)

I_{0}=\int\mathop{d^{D}p}\frac{1}{p^{2n}}=4\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}dpp^{D-1-2n}

should vanish as there is no mass parameter. However, we may have both IR (infrared) and UV (ultraviolet) divergences. Thus, we split up the above integral into two pieces as follows

(444)

\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\Lambda}dpp^{D-1-2n}}_{\text{IR}}+\underbrace{\int_{\Lambda}^{\infty}dpp^{D-1-2n}}_{\text{UV}}\xrightarrow[D\,=\,4+2\epsilon_{\text{IR}}]{D\,=\,4-2\epsilon_{\text{UV}}}\int_{0}^{\Lambda}dpp^{3-2n+2\epsilon_{\text{IR}}}+\int_{\Lambda}^{\infty}dpp^{3-2n-2\epsilon_{\text{UV}}},

where
\Lambda>0
.
Setting
\epsilon_{\text{UV}}=\epsilon_{\text{IR}}=\epsilon>0
leads to

(445)

\int_{0}^{\Lambda}dpp^{3-2n+2\epsilon_{\text{IR}}}+\int_{\Lambda}^{\infty}dpp^{3-2n-2\epsilon_{\text{UV}}}=\int_{0+}^{\infty}dpp^{3-2n-2\epsilon}\propto\epsilon\to 0

by dimensional regularization
[
16
]
.
For instance,
n=2
, we have that

(446)

\int_{0}^{\Lambda}dpp^{-1+2\epsilon_{\text{IR}}}+\int_{\Lambda}^{\infty}dpp^{-1-2\epsilon_{\text{UV}}}\propto\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\text{UV}}}-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{IR}}\right)=0.

The IR and UV divergences cancel each other.

Appendix 12.B
Finite temperature

In this section, we compute the diagram in Fig.
19
at finite temperature using the imaginary time formalism. Using the Feynman rules in Fig.
20
, we have that

(447)

I_{T}[D]=\frac{\lambda}{2}T\sum_{n}\int\frac{\mathop{d^{D-1}p}}{(2\pi)^{D-1}}\frac{1}{\omega_{n}^{2}+\mathbf{p}^{2}}.

We can express the sum as a contour integral
[
104
]

(448)

I_{T}[D]=\frac{\lambda}{2}\frac{1}{2\pi i}\ointctrclockwise_{C}dp_{0}\frac{1}{2}\int\frac{\mathop{d^{D-1}p}}{(2\pi)^{D-1}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{p}^{2}-p_{0}^{2}}\coth\left(\frac{p_{0}}{2T}\right),

where the contour
C
is shown in Fig.
21
.
We have used the Cauchy integral theorem. The poles of
\coth(p_{0}/2T)
are
2\pi iTn
, where
n
is an integer. Note that, the poles are equal to the Matsubara frequency in the imaginary axis
i\omega_{n}
.

Figure 21:
The counterclockwise contour
C
, which closes at infinite.

Using that

(449)

\frac{1}{2}\coth\left(\frac{x}{2}\right)=\frac{e^{x/2}+e^{-x/2}}{e^{x/2}-e^{-x/2}}=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{e^{x}-1}=-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{e^{-x}-1},

we obtain

(450)

\begin{split}I_{T}[D]=\frac{\lambda}{2}\frac{1}{2\pi i}&\Bigg[\int_{-i\infty+\eta}^{i\infty+\eta}dp_{0}\int\frac{\mathop{d^{D-1}p}}{(2\pi)^{D-1}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{p}^{2}-p_{0}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\exp(p_{0}/T)-1}\right)\\
&+\int_{i\infty-\eta}^{-i\infty-\eta}dp_{0}\int\frac{\mathop{d^{D-1}p}}{(2\pi)^{D-1}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{p}^{2}-p_{0}^{2}}\left(-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\exp(-p_{0}/T)-1}\right)\Bigg],\end{split}

where
\eta
is a small positive real number. We can simplify the above expression by changing the variable of the second integral in Eq. (
450
) to
-p_{0}
yielding

(451)

\begin{split}I_{T}[D]=\frac{\lambda}{2}\frac{1}{2\pi i}\Bigg[\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}dp_{0}\int\frac{\mathop{d^{D-1}p}}{(2\pi)^{D-1}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{p}^{2}-p_{0}^{2}}+\int_{-i\infty+\eta}^{i\infty+\eta}dp_{0}\int\frac{\mathop{d^{D-1}p}}{(2\pi)^{D-1}}\frac{2}{\mathbf{p}^{2}-p_{0}^{2}}N_{\text{B}}(p_{0})\Bigg],\end{split}

where
N_{\text{B}}({p_{0}})=(\exp(p_{0}/T)-1)^{-1}
is the Bose-Einstein distribution function.
Note that, this split up the integral Eq. (
447
) into two pieces. The first piece is temperature independent, which is equivalent to Eq. (
441
). It vanishes using dimensional regularization.
The second piece is temperature dependent.

To integrate
p_{0}
in Eq. (
451
), we use the Cauchy integral formula

(452)

\varointclockwise_{C^{\prime}}\mathop{dp_{0}}\frac{f(p_{0})}{\mathbf{p}^{2}-p_{0}^{2}}=-2\pi i\frac{f(|\mathbf{p}|)}{-2|\mathbf{p}|},

where the contour
C^{\prime}
is shown in Fig.
22
and
f(z)=2N_{\text{B}}{(z)}
. The sign on the right-hand side of Eq. (
452
) comes from the clockwise orientation of the contour used. The only contribution to the contour integral is from the pole
p_{0}=|\mathbf{p}|
.

Figure 22:
The contour
C^{\prime}
of integral in Eq. (
452
). The radius of the semicircle goes to infinity.

The temperature-dependent piece is then given by

(453)

I_{T}[D]=\frac{\lambda}{2}\int\frac{\mathop{d^{D-1}p}}{(2\pi)^{D-1}}\frac{1}{\omega}\frac{1}{e^{\omega/T}-1},

where we have used that
|\mathbf{p}|\equiv\omega
. This is valid since the mass of
\phi
vanishes
[
105
]
. The expression in Eq. (
453
) also holds for the massive theory with mass
M
. However, we would have that
\omega=\sqrt{\mathbf{p}^{2}+M^{2}}
. This shows that
\omega
is associated with the on-shell energy of the particle
\phi
.

Proceeding, we integrate over
p
in Eq. (
453
), which yields

(454)

I_{T}[D]=\frac{\lambda}{2}\left(\frac{2\pi^{(D-1)/2}}{\Gamma[(D-1)/2]}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathop{d\omega}}{(2\pi)^{D-1}}\frac{\omega^{D-3}}{e^{\omega/T}-1}\right),

where
\Gamma(x)
is the Gamma function.
Now, we should define
D=4-2\epsilon
to regularize the above integral. However, the integral in Eq. (
454
) is not divergent. Thus, we can set
D=4
from now on.

Upon the change of variable
\omega\to\omega T
, we obtain that

(455)

I_{T}[4]=\frac{\lambda}{2}\frac{T^{2}}{2\pi^{2}}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathop{d\omega}\frac{\omega^{D-3}}{e^{\omega}-1}}_{\Gamma(D-2)\zeta(D-2)}=\frac{\lambda}{2}\frac{T^{2}}{2\pi^{2}}\Gamma(2)\zeta(2)=\frac{\lambda}{24}T^{2},

where
\Gamma(2)=1
and
\zeta(2)=\pi^{2}/6
.
Although it vanishes at zero temperature as we have seen in Eq. (
442
), the tadpole-like diagram in Fig.
19
does not vanish at finite temperature.
